# Travel Hunter Pro — Current Handoff

## Google round-trip failure diagnostics — 2026-11-23

Failure results from the native Google round-trip path now preserve one allow-listed internal stage and bounded request/selection counters through the provider status envelope. A regression failed before this change and now passes; the focused native adapter suite passes 16/16. It confirms fixture behavior only. No live request was repeated, and the earlier live `invalid_response` remains unexplained because its stage was not captured. Google RPC permission/stability and round-trip cache live evidence remain open. [Evidence](docs/google-roundtrip-failure-stage-2026-11-23.md).

## ITA Matrix live adapter recheck — 2026-11-23

A bounded read-only YYZ–LHR one-way CAD request for 2027-02-12 returned 10 Matrix records and 10 accepted itineraries in 41,688 ms. An identical follow-up through `searchProviders` reported a durable cache hit with 10 accepted records; the normal flight worker validator returned `null`. This verifies one live query shape through the endpoint, provider pipeline and cache, but the cold response is slow and is not p95. No price or provider payload was retained. Matrix endpoint scope/rate terms, seller verification and broader live coverage remain open. [Evidence](docs/matrix-live-recheck-2026-11-23.md).

## Google native one-way exact-query cache — 2026-11-23

Validated Google one-way and count-party round-trip observations now reuse the durable provider cache. One-way uses a 60-second maximum TTL; round trip uses 30 seconds. Both are capped by quote freshness, bind exact route/dates/party/age/cabin/locale/filters, preserve original observation times and revalidate itineraries, airport-local timestamps against UTC/timezone evidence, and durations. Identified travelers, empty or stale results are excluded. A live one-way YYZ–LHR test returned 10 results in 5,083 ms and reused them in 3 ms. A bounded live round-trip check ended `invalid_response` with zero validated offers and was not retried; this is not evidence of no inventory. Focused tests pass 23/23, full `npm test` passes 693/693, and `npm run build` passes on retry after Next.js 16.4.3's security reminder. Google use scope, broader live stability, seller refresh and reverse-endpoint acceptance remain open. [Evidence](docs/google-oneway-response-cache-2026-11-23.md).

## Google calendar RPC — 2026-11-23

The native Google calendar path is enabled in local `.env.local` and `.env.example` for unfiltered one-adult economy round trips. A live fixed-return YYZ–LHR range populated two departures from one grid response; a five-night range needed two grids because the return dates differed. Both passed the calendar worker validator. One-way grid use is disabled because the shape is unsupported; a live one-day one-way query instead returned ten validated itinerary observations through exact search. Fatal provider failures now appear on the exact attempted date/route, while following dates remain unscanned. No prices or RPC captures were kept. Full tests pass 686/686 and the production build passes after retrying past Next.js's reminder. The moderate dependency advisory remains.

## 2026-11-23 — Ignav date-grid calendar fast path

Implemented a bounded Ignav `/fares/cheapest-dates` calendar fast path for eligible unfiltered count-party queries. It maps fixed and flexible stays to each exact return date, shares one grid response per stay length across departure-date cells, and reports wire responses separately from the unchanged logical search budget. Exact route/date/party/cabin/CAD/freshness/source coverage are checked before persistence. Results remain price-only observations; the UI directs a date selection to an exact Ignav search. Filters, child parties and identified travelers stay on the exact itinerary path.

**Evidence:** one-way, fixed/flexible round-trip, filter fallback, tamper rejection and worker fixture tests pass. A live YYZ–LHR two-date query returned two unverified CAD rows; both the calendar aggregator and an isolated durable worker accepted the exact cells. Full `npm test` is **686/686**. A production-build browser fixture rendered Ignav/Matrix price-only values beside a fare and verified exact-search source handoff, with zero page errors and external requests blocked. `npm run build` passed after retrying past Next.js 16.4.3's security reminder. `npm audit --audit-level=low` reports one moderate Next.js advisory; npm's forced fix is outside the declared range. Live evidence is one query shape, not a latency claim or broad schema proof. Details: [Ignav calendar evidence](docs/ignav-cheapest-dates-calendar-2026-11-23.md).

**Next:** verify the endpoint under the configured Ignav account and rate policy; document the live query scope/schema; add a fresh live observation only if access is authorized. Continue provider-by-provider reverse endpoint work and `work.md` §17. Do not represent date-level observations as itineraries or confirmed seller prices. No dependency upgrade was done.

## Ignav fare-calendar cache — 2026-11-23

Calendar workers now pass the shared SQLite provider cache into exact provider searches, extending Ignav's existing exact-query cache to repeated fare-calendar searches across jobs/workers. The cache preserves the original observation timestamp and reports a hit; it still excludes identified parties, infants, empty results, stale observations and invalid/mismatched offers. Regression was red before the wiring and passes across two independent persistent store connections. Focused calendar/provider tests pass 71/71; full `npm test` passes 680/680. `npm run build` passes after retrying past Next.js 16.4.3's security reminder. No live request or real latency measurement was made. See [dated evidence](docs/ignav-calendar-cache-2026-11-23.md). Broader reverse-endpoint and §17 work remains incomplete.

## Skyscanner polling budget hardening — 2026-11-23

The partner adapter now validates poll configuration before dispatch, caps search and refresh at eight poll operations, enforces a 1–10 s configured interval, and reports poll operations used/remaining. Fully validated offers from incomplete poll snapshots are published through provider progress before polling finishes; route aggregation accepts the offer under worker validation, later snapshots replace prior route state, and final output remains authoritative. A zero-poll budget returns any initial partial offers with explicit partial coverage. Skyscanner/provider tests pass 31/31; route streaming regression passes 2/2; full `npm test` passes 678/678. Production build passed on the unchanged application code after retrying Next.js's security reminder. `SKYSCANNER_API_KEY` is absent here, so this is fixture evidence only, not live partner verification.

## All-source Matrix partial-result forwarding — 2026-11-23

Fixed a streaming gap in `searchProviders`: the bounded all-source fan-out had removed `onProviderResult` before calling each adapter, so ITA Matrix's existing partial API/browser updates were discarded and only its final result reached the durable job. All-source fan-out now forwards the callback with the correct source index/count; route progress replaces that provider's partial snapshot with the final snapshot when Matrix completes. Added a regression that failed before the fix and verifies both partial and final Matrix events. Focused provider regressions pass 2/2; full `npm test` passes 674/674; production build passes after retrying Next.js's security reminder. No provider call or UI latency check was made for this fix. [Plan and source map](docs/reverse-endpoint-implementation.md).

One live YHZ→LAX Dec 16, one-adult/economy query returned 12 validator-accepted offers in 3,893 ms. ## Google native one-way adapter and durable UI dispatch — 2026-11-23

Google's API Anything one-way `search`/`top` projection still lacks per-segment identity and response-side currency, so the app continues to withhold those route-only rows and never infers CAD from `curr=CAD`. A read-only native results-page adapter supports one-adult economy one-way searches. It makes at most one `GetShoppingResults` fare request, validates exact route/date, visible one-passenger and CAD labels, complete segment identity, airport-local chronology and a maximum of 80 rows, and describes output as an unrefreshed results-page observation rather than a seller quote. The shared RPC matcher was corrected so request caps count both Playwright route requests and response objects.

End-to-end verification found and fixed a bypass: exact-airport Google one-way requests in `executeFlightSearch` still called the old API Anything parser instead of the provider adapter. After routing that branch through `searchProviders`, one loopback website search (YHZ→LAX, 2026-12-15, one adult, economy, Google only) completed through the durable worker and rendered 10 Google offer cards with zero browser errors. That success is not stable evidence: two subsequent UI runs for 2026-12-15 and 2026-12-17 returned `invalid_response`; a bounded diagnostic identified “no native RPC data,” and no offers rendered. No CAPTCHA/human check appeared. A guarded reproducible live smoke script reports source status, offer count, first-card/complete latency and browser errors without recording fares. A visible no-flights statement on the exact CAD/one-adult page now completes without an RPC; empty or malformed RPC data remains an error unless the page explicitly states no results. This new no-RPC branch is fixture-verified, not live-observed. Keep Google one-way live reliability open until successful repeat evidence and source scope are established. Fixture tests cover exact segments, CAD/party evidence, explicit no-results with/without RPC, empty RPC rejection, access refusal and the one-request cap. Other parties/cabins, seller refresh, baggage and final charges remain open. Full `npm test` passes **673/673**; production build passes after the Next.js reminder retry; audit reports one moderate Next.js advisory whose forced fix is outside the declared range. [Implementation/evidence](docs/google-api-anything-one-way-gap-2026-11-23.md).

## Successful-response quota exhaustion guard — 2026-11-23

`lib/source-fetch.mjs` now reads standardized remaining/reset headers on successful responses. If the provider reports zero requests left and a valid future reset, the current response remains available to the adapter while the shared SQLite policy defers later calls across jobs/workers. The regression confirms a second worker on an independent SQLite connection is blocked. The first full run uncovered and fixed compatibility with Ignav's numeric deterministic test clock. Verification: source-fetch **15/15**, combined source-fetch/Ignav **39/39**, full `npm test` **663/663**, production build passed after the Next.js security reminder retry. `npm audit --audit-level=low` still reports one moderate Next.js 16.4.3 advisory; fix requires an out-of-range forced upgrade. No live provider call was made for this change. [Evidence](docs/provider-quota-exhaustion-2026-11-23.md).

The broader reverse-endpoint goal remains incomplete. The `.env.local` presence check found Ignav and Omkar credentials, but Expedia Travel Redirect, Skyscanner, Air Canada NDC, and Booking Demand credentials are absent. The observed Omkar response still does not establish CAD or absolute segment chronology; check its allowed account usage/reset before another live request. Matrix and Google remain unofficial web-app RPCs with access-scope and runtime-evidence limits. Continue implementation only where provider access and terms permit, and retain unconfigured/unsupported sources as open.

## Shared 5xx source cooldown — 2026-11-23

The shared HTTP transport now retries safe GET/HEAD calls at most once, then records a durable 30-second provider cooldown after an unhinted HTTP 5xx; a longer provider `Retry-After` takes precedence. POST is not replayed. Regional calendars suppress a provider across remaining route pairs after a 5xx while continuing healthy providers and preserving partial-coverage labels. Query-local failures without an HTTP 5xx still allow other routes. Focused transport/calendar tests pass **67/67**, full `npm test` passes **661/661**, and `npm run build` passes after retrying Next.js 16.4.3's security reminder. `npm audit --audit-level=low` reports one moderate Next.js 16.4.3 advisory; the suggested 16.4.6 fix is outside the declared range. No live provider request was made. [Evidence](docs/source-5xx-cooldown-2026-11-23.md).

## ITA Matrix price-only calendar observations — 2026-11-23

Unfiltered exact-route/date fare calendars can now retain an ITA Matrix-reported group total as a separate price-only observation. Acceptance verifies CAD, exact amount, party counts, cabin, one-way or fixed-return dates, observation time, and the encoded Matrix search state in the source URL. Any active filter suppresses this total because the observed group total does not prove filter compliance. The calendar explicitly labels it as not a fare quote and does not place it in the itinerary price field; “Search flights” returns to Matrix for Matrix observations. The durable worker repeats these checks and requires successful Matrix coverage for that route.

Focused calendar/query/worker tests pass **57/57**, full `npm test` passes **657/657**, and `npm run build` passes. A production-build browser fixture displays the Matrix price-only calendar card beside itinerary results in partial and completed states; it uses intercepted provider/job traffic. No live Matrix query was made. Full repository acceptance and live/provider verification remain open. [Evidence](docs/matrix-price-only-calendar-2026-11-23.md).

## Google calendar grids for multiple airport pairs — 2026-11-23

The opt-in Google date-grid adapter now handles bounded multi-airport/region calendars when Google is selected alone or alongside other providers in an all-source search. It issues one exact-route grid per airport pair and reuses its date rows, while request budgeting counts every logical date/pair and `wireResponses` reports actual grid calls. Google remains limited to one adult, economy, no identified party, no active filters, and one-way or fixed-return trips; variable-return round trips and filtered price-only scans use other paths. The all-source path keeps each other configured provider's ordinary adapter and accepts Google price-only rows only with matching Google route coverage.

The prior browser fixture covers generic calendar streaming, not this source combination. See [Google multi-airport evidence](docs/google-calendar-multi-airport-2026-11-23.md). Overall reverse-endpoint work and §17 remain open.

## ITA Matrix progressive detail results — 2026-11-23

Matrix now publishes a validated preview after its initial response, then updates the same source result as each matched detail response arrives. Early CAD observations can display as price-only while flight details are being fetched; fully validated itineraries replace their corresponding observations. Fixed the single-airport callback-shape bug that dropped provider updates, the worker's `origin/destination` versus `from/to` route match for price-only observations, and premature route-complete status during a partial Matrix response.

Verification: Matrix/provider/location suites **54/54**; full `npm test` **653/653**; production build passed after the Next.js reminder retry; rebuilt fixture UI showed a Matrix price-only card plus another source's partial result **437 ms** after persisted progress, followed by final fixture results, zero browser errors and no external requests. No live Matrix request was made; live progressive behavior/latency remains open. Details: [Matrix progressive evidence](docs/matrix-progressive-results-2026-11-23.md). Broader endpoint implementation and §17 remain incomplete.

## Ignav exact-query response cache — 2026-11-23

Added a durable SQLite response cache for validated Ignav flight offers. It keys on exact route, dates/trip type, count party and exact child ages, cabin, filters, market, currency, and a hash of the configured Ignav account scope. It only caches positive, fully accepted, currently fresh CAD results for count-based parties; identified travelers, infants, missing child ages, empty results, and invalid/stale offers bypass storage. TTL is at most five minutes and never extends the quote's original freshness window. Cache hits preserve each offer's original `observedAt` and the UI labels them “cached observed results.” Storage is capped at 100 entries and 512 KB per result. This is Ignav-only; no cache policy has been inferred for other providers.

Verification: focused cache/store/provider/search tests **40/40**; full `npm test` **649/649**; `npm run build` passed after retrying past Next.js's security reminder; `npm run check:flight-streaming` passed against an isolated production build with fixture traffic intercepted (partial/final rendering in 369 ms, cached label asserted, zero browser errors, external requests blocked); `git diff --check` clean. No live provider request was made to force a cache hit, so live warm-hit behavior remains unverified. `npm audit --audit-level=low` reports one moderate vulnerability in Next.js 16.4.3; the available fix is outside the declared dependency range. Broader reverse-endpoint work and §17 acceptance remain open.

Cache-integrity follow-up: reuse now also requires the cached normalized query envelope to match the full exact request. Malformed cache rows are evicted and trigger a fresh provider search. Focused cache/store/provider tests pass **37/37**; full suite **650/650**; build passed after the Next.js reminder retry. No live request was made. This only strengthens Ignav cache correctness; source-level and overall acceptance gaps remain open.

## Expedia via Omkar live recheck and diagnostics — 2026-11-23

The configured Omkar wrapper returned HTTP 200 for one read-only YHZ–LAX, 2026-12-10, one-adult/economy query: 80 flight rows, 0 verified offers, 0 price-only observations, and 227 aggregate route/fare rejections (the pre-diagnostics count did not distinguish them). ## ACV structured adult multi-room support — 2026-11-23

Extended the ordinary-widget structured response path to adult-only multi-room searches. A live YHZ–BCN query for two adults and two rooms exposed `numberOfRooms=2`, `r0=1-adult-0`, and `r1=1-adult-0` in the rendered listing URL. The provider returned 20 package rows; normalizer checks showed that multiplying each per-passenger row total by its passenger count reconciled the package total for all 20/20 rows. The adapter then accepted 20/20, retained the two exact room allocations and passed `packageWorkerResultError`. This is live adapter plus validator evidence, not a durable website/worker run. No property names, prices or response bodies were logged. The UI now shows provider-listed room allocations.

Added `lib/vacation-room-allocation.mjs` to parse contiguous `r0..rN` room entries with at least one adult per room, validate exact aggregate adult count, and reject missing, duplicated, malformed, or child-bearing allocations on the multi-room path. The normalizer and durable worker bind those allocations to the exact rendered listing and keep child+multi-room requests unsupported. Regression coverage verifies weighted per-passenger totals, missing room entries and tampered occupancy evidence. Focused package suites passed **40/40**; full `npm test` passed **641/641**; `npm run build` passed after retrying past Next.js's security reminder. `npm audit --audit-level=low` reports one moderate vulnerability in installed Next.js 16.4.3 (affected range 16.0.0–16.4.4); no dependency upgrade was applied because the available fix is outside the declared range. The prompted upgrade choice remains pending.

Still open: child allocations across multiple rooms, infants in the structured path, a durable website/UI result for this multi-room shape, pages beyond 20, complete timezone-validated package segments, final checkout availability, all mandatory/local fees, and seller-confirmed pricing. See [ACV endpoint evidence](docs/acv-structured-package-endpoint-discovery-2026-11-14.md).

## ACV structured child party support — 2026-11-23

Extended the fast ordinary-widget JSON path to support one actual-age child (2–17) with adults in a single room; infants and multi-child parties remain on the rendered-card path pending schema evidence. The API request must carry exactly one `child-ages` value matching the validated query, the rendered provider listing must match `non_adult1` and the explicit `r0` adult/child allocation, and `pricing.perPaxType` must contain adult (`A`) and child (`C`) rows whose passenger counts match the submitted party and whose tax-inclusive exact-cent totals reconcile to the package group total. The durable worker validates the same child ages and row counts before persistence. Synthetic contract tests pass; a fresh ordinary-widget adapter run for one adult plus exact age 7 returned 20 records, accepted 20/20, and passed `packageWorkerResultError` with no rejection. This is live adapter plus validator evidence; the website/worker UI could not be rerun because Next.js 16.4.3 stopped `next dev` with a security-upgrade prompt. No package prices/property names or response body were logged. Focused tests passed 36/36 and full `npm test` passed 637/637 after moving stale ITA Matrix fixture dates into the future. `npm run build` passed after retrying past Next.js's security reminder. `npm audit --audit-level=low` reports one moderate Next.js vulnerability (16.0.0–16.4.4; installed 16.4.3); the available upgrade is outside the declared dependency range. No dependency upgrade has been applied.

The path remains bounded to one 20-result page, one room, one child, and no infants. Itinerary entries are journey summaries without complete offset-aware segment chronology; per-room allocation for adults in multiple rooms, exact room/property match, checkout availability, local charges and seller-confirmed totals remain open. The prompted Next security upgrade has not been run. See [ACV response discovery and evidence](docs/acv-structured-package-endpoint-discovery-2026-11-14.md).

## ACV structured package adapter hardening — 2026-11-14

Continued the ordinary-widget `GET /packages-search/search` adapter in `lib/vacation-search.mjs`, `lib/vacation-normalize.mjs`, and the durable package worker guard. Live schema checks corrected the request cabin code to `Y`, matched hotel `destinationId` to the request's `dest` field, validated the provider's inclusive zero-based `currentEnd`, accepted the observed adult-only pax marker `A` only alongside matching adult counts, and updated journey cabin code validation to `Y`. The worker now cross-checks destination evidence and adult pax rows. A current local website-to-worker search completed through `structured_widget_response`: 20/20 records accepted and rendered, CAD visible, 78 total listings indicated with more beyond the first page. Persisted job duration was 24.955 s; this includes full job processing and is one sample, not a latency guarantee. Latest focused package/normalizer/worker tests passed 34/34. Full `npm test` passed 635/635 before these final adapter refinements; final full verification is in progress.

This closes the live-path gap for the supported adult-only, one-room, first-page adapter shape, not P6 overall. The normal rendered-card path remains for children, infants and 40-result queries. ACV itinerary entries remain journey summaries rather than a timezone-validated complete segment chain. Package totals remain estimates; multi-room party allocation, exact property address/room, checkout availability, all local charges and seller-confirmed totals remain open. ## Google Flights date-grid integration — 2026-11-14

Implemented the ordinary-UI `GetCalendarPicker` response path through `lib/google-calendar.mjs`, calendar dispatch, durable worker validation, and `app/FareCalendar.js`. It supports one adult/economy/one airport pair/no filters for one-way or a fixed-return round trip. Exact query, source URL, CAD, dates, return date, price-only scope and observation timestamp are validated; UI follow-up keeps the same trip type and return date. One response is reused across its departure-date range (up to 31 dates), while logical provider/date budget and actual `wireResponses` remain distinct. The adapter remains opt-in (`TRAVEL_GOOGLE_CALENDAR_RPC=1`).

A bounded live ordinary YHZ–LAX round-trip date-picker request for Dec 1–8, 2026 with fixed return Dec 8 returned all 8 requested date rows, each priced, and the UI visibly confirmed CAD. This is a date-level observation only: no flight segments, fare rules, baggage, seller availability or final charges were returned. A separate one-way attempt failed closed with numeric marker 4; no meaning is inferred. API Anything's `priceCalendar` internal-error-13 path remains separate and unusable.

Verification after these changes: focused parser/calendar/worker tests 61/61; full `TMPDIR=/dev/shm npm test` 631/631; `npm run build` passed; `npm audit` found 0 vulnerabilities. The round-trip shape has a current live response; one-way reliability and unattended source scope remain open. See [endpoint inventory](docs/reverse-endpoint-implementation.md), [all-source implementation map](docs/reverse-endpoint-full-implementation-plan.md), and [dated evidence](docs/reverse-endpoint-progressive-results-2026-11-14.md#google-flights-date-grid-integration--2026-11-14).

## ACV suggestion endpoint parse bound — 2026-11-14

Added a 1 MB declared/decoded response cap and fail-closed JSON parsing to the Air Canada Vacations widget's structured location-suggestions endpoint (`lib/vacation-suggestions.mjs`). Repeated exact lookups now use a process-local 5-minute cache capped at 100 entries; cache hits preserve the original observation time and destination keys include the selected origin. Focused `tests/vacation-suggestions.test.mjs` passes 10/10, including cache eviction. Final-tree `npm test` passes 619/619 (89.6s); the production build and audit (0 vulnerabilities) passed after implementation changes. No live provider request was made. The browser session remains the ordinary observed flow; no Authorization token was extracted or replayed. Playwright may buffer the response before the application cap. Package listing responses remain unbounded at the page level. Detailed note: [ACV suggestion response bound](docs/acv-suggestion-response-bound-2026-11-14.md).

The broader fast reverse-endpoint goal remains active and incomplete. The ordinary-UI `GetCalendarPicker` adapter now has a current successful fixed-return round-trip response, while a separate one-way query still failed closed. ACV direct backend access still requires an approved server-auth contract. Partner credentials, provider-specific rate records, live schema/drift checks, browser response bounds and cross-job performance evidence remain open. The current all-source map is [here](docs/reverse-endpoint-full-implementation-plan.md).

## Reverse-endpoint fan-out and progressive flight results — 2026-11-14

Implemented bounded all-source fan-out (default two concurrent adapters), source-by-source durable progress publication and persistence timestamps, validated partial flight previews, progressive normal-flight UI rendering, and bounded provider timing metadata. Shared HTTP requests expose sanitized per-provider latency histograms; nested adapter contexts roll into flight/calendar summaries, and regional progress keeps immutable snapshots. Google Flights native RPCs now have a distinct per-query latency histogram; this covers only measured native calls, not browser startup, other browser-based sources, or cross-job p95. Booking.com Demand API uses shared durable pacing/cooldown, honors Retry-After, does not automatically retry POSTs, and caps each decoded response at 2 MB. Ignav/Expedia/Skyscanner/Omkar JSON and Air Canada NDC XML bodies are also size-bounded. ITA Matrix API result streams are capped at 5 MB; native browser result/detail parsers reject over-limit bodies after checking Content-Length, though Playwright may buffer the body first. ACV currency-terms HTML has a 2 MB decoded-body cap; package/widget browser operations and Booking.com page searches now use shared source pacing and persist auth/rate cooldowns, while package/Booking page responses still lack uniform byte limits. Unsupported traveler shapes remain source-specific statuses. Latest verification: Google native suite 11/11; hotel/ACV browser suites 43/43; `npm test` 616/616; `npm run build` passed; `npm audit --audit-level=low` found 0 vulnerabilities. The prior local production streaming check was 387 ms; the latest dev-stack fixture recheck was 454 ms persistence-to-UI, with three polls, provider calls blocked and zero browser errors. These are code/fixture checks, not live inventory or production latency guarantees. User-reported source permission is acknowledged; endpoint/account scope, missing partner credentials, other-source pacing decisions, and end-to-end p95 remain open. [Details](docs/reverse-endpoint-progressive-results-2026-11-14.md).

## Reverse-endpoint feasibility follow-up — 2026-11-14

The broader goal of moving most eligible sources to fast, fully implemented structured endpoints remains active. The current code already uses structured integrations for several sources; it does not have authorization, credentials or provider evidence to complete every listed adapter. A CAD-configured call to the bundled API Anything Google `priceCalendar` operation returned batchexecute internal error 13 twice for YYZ–LHR 2026-12-01, so no date prices or CAD behavior were verified and the operation remains unwired. Air Canada Vacations' direct suggestions endpoint returned 401 without auth; its ordinary widget request succeeded and included an Authorization header. No token value was observed or retained; the normal widget flow remains. The direct 401 activated the shared source cooldown. Continue with a fresh normal Google Flights calendar interaction after access is available, and obtain the provider-approved ACV server-auth contract before implementing direct suggestions. Do not retry against ACV before the returned cooldown, reuse session credentials, or represent fixture behavior as live data. See [feasibility evidence and adapter status](docs/reverse-endpoint-progressive-results-2026-11-14.md#reverse-endpoint-feasibility-checks--2026-11-14) and [source inventory](docs/reverse-endpoint-implementation.md).

ACV widget and Booking.com browser operations now reserve the shared per-source gap across worker processes; observed auth/rate refusals store cooldowns, close the browser, and expose retry time through the hotel job result. The focused hotel/ACV suites pass **43/43** for pacing, `Retry-After`, refusal state, and cleanup. This hardens existing browser flows; it does not implement a direct ACV package/suggestions RPC or establish the providers' external quotas. The direct RPC access/auth and Google CAD date-calendar integration remain open. Updated [endpoint contract](docs/reverse-endpoint-implementation.md) and [evidence](docs/reverse-endpoint-progressive-results-2026-11-14.md).

Initial audit against the then-current production build used Chromium on loopback with external requests blocked. ## P10 official hotel-engine access recheck — 2026-11-14

The current implementation prices official properties through Reservit only. Expected Booking.com Demand variables `BOOKING_API_TOKEN` and `BOOKING_AFFILIATE_ID` are absent from `.env.local` (values were not read); no Cloudbeds/Mews production credentials or property authorization are configured. ## P11/P12 YYZ–Europe live calendar and SQLite status-poll contention fix — 2026-11-13 00:36–00:41 UTC

- A real local-worker all-source one-way calendar query searched YYZ→Europe for Dec 15 2026, one adult/economy, four destination candidates and a 16-request cap. The resolver selected FRA/FCO/BCN/MUC and reported 839 known omitted candidates, exclusions LHR/CDG/MAD, and 261 geographic exclusions. All four selected airport pairs received requests.
- The best accepted result was an ITA Matrix CAD 516.62 price-only YYZ–FRA observation at 00:37:17 UTC. Ignav, Google Flights and Matrix returned responses on all four pairs; Expedia via Omkar returned HTTP 500 on all four; Expedia partner, Skyscanner and Air Canada NDC were unconfigured. All 16 request slots were used, but `fullyScanned:false`, `datesChecked:0`; this is one date and four pairs, not exhaustive Europe coverage. Full detail: [dated calendar evidence](docs/calendar-multi-airport-live-2026-11-13.md).
- One owner-bound status poll returned `database is locked` while the worker was writing. The job completed at 00:39:42 UTC, but the one-off monitor had not retained its owner token; read-only SQLite inspection recovered only the query and result summary. Health subsequently returned `ok`, one live worker. Investigation found `get()` forced global expiry deletes and unconditional updates on every poll. It now throttles retention sweeps, expires the requested terminal row immediately when necessary, and skips updates unless that job's deadline/lease expired. `findFlightOffer()` no longer forces the sweep. The regression failed before and passed after; `node --test tests/job-store.test.mjs` passed 18/18, full `npm test` passed 541/541, and the isolated production build passed. SQLite busy/locked errors now return a generic HTTP 503 with `Retry-After: 1` rather than HTTP 400/raw DB text. `npm audit fix` moved Hono to 4.13.18; audit reported zero vulnerabilities.
- The post-change audit surfaced a moderate `hono@4.13.14` advisory through API Anything's MCP SDK. `npm audit fix` updated the compatible lockfile resolution to 4.13.18; audit then reported zero vulnerabilities. The isolated production build passed after the dependency update and final error-mapping code change; a final `npm audit --audit-level=low` reported zero vulnerabilities. The local site returned HTTP 200 and same-origin health returned `ok`, one live worker, empty queue, and zero expired leases/deadlines at 00:53:34 UTC. P12 remains local-only; this fix reduces unnecessary SQLite writes but does not prove contention can never happen.

## P4 Booking.com Demand API multi-room Flight + Hotel subtotal — 2026-11-08

- Connected the existing Demand API product/price checks to `compareFlightHotel`. Multi-room subtotals now require exact room allocation and child-age echoes, each provider product total in CAD cents, an exact product-sum/expected-total match, matching property/destination/stay, and fresh flight/hotel/property observations. A one-cent difference may remain an individual provider observation but is explicitly marked as not an exact total match and cannot form a combined subtotal. ## P9 shared Matrix API response cache — 2026-11-08

- Replaced the process-local Matrix API response `Map` with a shared SQLite cache in `.cache/jobs/search.sqlite`. Successful normalized API responses have a five-minute TTL. Cache keys are SHA-256 digests of the validated request and Matrix API key; the raw key is not persisted. The cache is bounded to 100 entries and 1 MB per response, purges expired/corrupt entries, and marks returned source rows `cached: true`. Browser-based Matrix family/infant searches still bypass this API cache.

## P2 age-only round-trip boundary correction — 2026-11-07

- Re-audit found age-only checks in `lib/travelers.mjs` could reject trips well before a traveler could possibly reach the supplier adult-fare category. For an age-11 child, the old threshold treated any positive stay as a possible birthday crossing; for younger children it could reject roughly one year early.
- Added a shared conservative minimum-days calculation: with age-only input the next birthday may be tomorrow, and each further birthday is at least 365 days later. Applied it consistently to Ignav, Expedia, Google round-trip, and Matrix count-party preflight. Exact-DOB identified-party validation remains date-specific.
- Added regressions showing an age-10 trip of 365 days remains eligible for preflight while 366 days is blocked for DOB confirmation; an age-11 traveler is blocked when a next-day return could cross the boundary. Focused traveler/Expedia/Google/Matrix tests passed 48/48; full `npm test` passed 466/466 (85.2s), `npm run build` passed, and `npm audit --audit-level=low` found zero vulnerabilities.
- No live source query was made. Supplier-specific fare acceptance remains unknown; the correction only avoids impossible false-positive blocks and still requires exact DOBs whenever category crossing is possible. P2 remains **Partial**.

## P11 calendar airport coverage and budgeted pair disclosure — 2026-11-07

- Raised exact-calendar airport candidate capacity from 10 to 20 per location, matching the regional flight-search selector. Added an accessible `Airport candidates per location` control (1–20; default 10), persisted the setting with restored jobs, and made it part of the calendar query identity.
- Calendar APIs now accept a bounded 20×20 candidate set and defer over-budget pruning to the worker. The worker preserves supplied candidate order, reduces airport candidates until all planned route/date requests fit the selected maximum of 250, and records every known candidate-pair omitted for budget in `coverage.airportPairsUncheckedByBudget`. It also lists exact airport/date pairs that never started after a source stop, with a reason. The calendar UI displays both kinds of omissions. Query dates, return dates, party, cabin, source, and filters still pass unchanged to each selected provider.
- Added regressions for 20 candidates per side, 400 submitted pairs, budget clipping (250 cap; 240 selected in the fixture), 160 exact omitted pairs, invalid 21-candidate/limit inputs, request enqueue, source-stop pair coverage, and calendar-key identity. Focused calendar/search-endpoint tests passed 38/38. Final `npm test` passed 453/453 (94.4s); `npm run build` passed; `npm audit --audit-level=low` found 0 vulnerabilities. Updated the Chromium check for the current flight form and fixture-intercepted capability preflight; it blocks service workers, intercepts job creation with a controlled 503, and asserts one intercepted submission. The rebuilt page passed at `127.0.0.1:3002` with provider/job work intercepted; it did not reach a fare provider. Current page and same-origin health both return HTTP 200 / `ok`; at `2026-11-07T18:57:55.840Z`, queue depth was 0, one worker was live, no leases/deadlines expired, and three stale/three stopped historical rows remained.
- An earlier browser check unintentionally reached the configured Ignav worker at `2026-11-07T18:43:59Z`. The durable record was cancelled at `18:45:26Z` with no result retained after 22 logical fare requests started and 21 airport/date queries checked. Physical HTTP attempts may exceed the logical count due to source retries/polls. This was not feature acceptance evidence; the worker returned to queue 0 and no seller handoff or booking occurred.
- This broadens bounded candidate coverage; it does not prove repeatable live calendar fares, provider filter enforcement, seller verification, or exhaustive geographic/airport coverage. P11 and §17 remain **Partial**.

## P12 local browser security headers and nonce CSP — 2026-11-07

- Added response security headers for every route in `next.config.mjs`: `nosniff`, `DENY` framing, strict-origin referrer policy, disabled camera/microphone/geolocation, and same-origin opener isolation.
- Added `proxy.js` to create a cryptographically random, per-response nonce for rendered pages and apply a strict CSP. Production script policy uses self + nonce + `strict-dynamic`, with no `unsafe-inline` or `unsafe-eval`; CSS also uses the nonce. CSP is excluded from API/static-asset routes. The root layout now waits for a request so Next can attach nonces to rendered scripts. Removed the only inline style attribute and moved it to CSS.
- Added `tests/security-headers.test.mjs` for policy/headers and extended the controlled flight browser check to verify nonced page scripts and zero CSP console errors. Focused security/API-boundary tests passed 3/3. Full `npm test` passed 452/452 (131.8s); `npm run build` passed; `npm audit --audit-level=low` found 0 vulnerabilities. Rebuilt-loopback flight browser check passed (zero page/CSP errors, provider/job requests intercepted). Page and health API returned HTTP 200 at `127.0.0.1:3001`; health at `2026-11-07T18:13:09.621Z` reported `ok`, queue 0, one live worker, no expired work, and three stale/three stopped historical worker rows.
- P12 remains **Partial** and the app stays loopback-only: this does not add user authentication, encrypted credential storage/rotation, deployment identity, comprehensive per-user abuse controls, external monitoring/alerting, HTTPS termination/HSTS, backup recovery drills, or public security review. Exact next action before public deployment: complete those controls in the selected hosting environment and test them there; do not expose this local app publicly.

## P3 separate-ticket timestamp validation — 2026-11-07

- `lib/separate-tickets.mjs` now rejects timezone-less values in fields declared as UTC. Departure and arrival instants must be real timestamps with `Z` or an explicit offset within ±14:00; local wall-clock fields must be real timezone-free timestamps. Segment airport codes, positive duration, airport continuity, and chronological order are checked before baseline matching or separate-ticket comparison. Exact-flight matching uses the same strict instant parser.
- Added a regression proving a timezone-less UTC field cannot authorize a baseline (`tests/separate-tickets.test.mjs`). The first full-suite run caught an unrelated nondeterministic FIFO test: equal millisecond timestamps fell through to random UUID ordering. `tests/job-store.test.mjs` now gives sequential jobs deterministic timestamps so the test's ordering assumption is explicit.

## Booking.com city verification before search — 2026-11-07

## SQLite job database backup/restore — 2026-11-07

- Added `lib/job-database-backup.mjs` and `scripts/job-database.mjs`, exposed as `npm run jobs:database -- <backup|verify|restore> ...`. Backups use SQLite `VACUUM INTO` so committed WAL data is included, then run full integrity, foreign-key and required-schema checks. Output files use mode `0600`; a newly created parent directory uses `0700`. A hard link publishes the completed copy without overwriting an existing destination. CLI output contains only operation metadata.
- Restore always writes to a new file. It converts queued/running/paused jobs into terminal errors, removes leases and hotel challenge image state, closes pending/inflight interactions through the job-store trigger, and reports the number interrupted. This prevents a restored queue from automatically repeating provider searches or reviving a stale CAPTCHA. Completed observations are retained. Operators must stop website and worker before changing `TRAVEL_JOB_DB` and restarting.
- Test-first public CLI coverage verifies a live WAL snapshot, completed-result/owner persistence, interrupted queued and running work, cleared hotel challenge state, pending-interaction closure, no automatic claim, mode `0600`, no search/challenge/owner data in CLI output, refusal to overwrite, same-source/destination rejection, and corrupt-file rejection. Focused test: `node --test tests/job-database-backup.test.mjs` passed 2/2. Full `npm test` passed 429/429 (131.7 seconds); `npm run build` passed; `npm audit --audit-level=low` found 0 vulnerabilities.
- At `2026-11-07T14:08:57Z`, loopback root and `/api/health` returned HTTP 200. Health was `ok`, queue depth 0, one live worker, zero expired leases/deadlines, with three stale and three stopped historical worker records. The database CLI usage path was also checked. No supplier search ran for this backup work.
- This adds local single-disk recovery capability, not an automated schedule, encrypted/offsite backups, external monitoring, restore drill with operational data, multi-user identity, or public deployment readiness. Database backups contain sensitive ages, searches and observations; filesystem permissions are not encryption. Keep the app local-only and protect copies separately. P12 and §17 Worker health remain **Partial**.

## Per-owner and rolling job-start limits — 2026-11-07

- The durable job store now allows at most five active jobs per owner capability while retaining the existing global 20-job queue ceiling. Queued, running and paused work counts toward the owner's cap; idempotent retries return the original job, and terminal jobs release capacity. The jobs API returns HTTP 429 with a user-readable message when the owner limit is reached.
- The durable store also limits new job starts to 10 per owner and 30 across the shared SQLite database in a rolling 60-second window. Events survive app restarts; idempotent retries do not consume a slot; a rejected start returns HTTP 429 with `Retry-After`. The existing five-active-per-owner and 20-active-global ceilings still apply. This global cap also limits evasion by generating fresh owner capabilities.
- Red/green coverage in `tests/job-store.test.mjs` proves the rolling window, owner/global boundaries, expiry and persistence across store restart. `tests/search-jobs-route.test.mjs` verifies HTTP 429, idempotent retry, owner isolation and `Retry-After` through the actual route handler. Focused tests passed 21/21 (17 store + 4 route); the first full run had one regional calendar worker deadline failure under suite load, the specific test passed alone, and the subsequent full `npm test` passed 427/427. `npm run build` passed, `npm audit --audit-level=low` found 0 vulnerabilities, and rebuilt-site `check:search-settings` plus `check:route-settings` passed with provider calls intercepted. After restarting the built website and worker, `2026-11-07T13:30:23.662Z` health was `ok`, queue 0, one live worker, zero expired leases/deadlines, with three stale and three stopped historical worker rows; root returned HTTP 200.
- These are durable local job-start/concurrency limits, not authentication or a general per-route quota. They apply to processes sharing this SQLite database; a public multi-instance deployment still needs shared identity, distributed quotas, alerting and security review. Keep the app local-only.

## Europe airport geography aligned with UN M49 — 2026-11-06

- Changed the Europe candidate resolver to start from the live official UNSD M49 Europe (region 150) country/area set, map those ISO codes to Wikidata country entities, then evaluate each airport coordinate against the documented approximate continental box (25°W–45°E, 34°N–72°N). This avoids using a country’s continent claim alone to pull every airport in a transcontinental country into Europe. Airport coordinates are the deciding scope at the transcontinental edge; airports without valid coordinates are withheld.
- The resolver includes airport-class subclasses, merges/deduplicates OurAirports and Wikidata candidates, orders by recorded Wikidata passenger traffic (unmeasured entries follow alphabetically), reports and excludes the top three candidates, and starts fare candidates at rank four. The policy version is `UNSD-M49-2026-10+TH-2026-11+airport-box-v1+dual-directory-v2`; the shared region cache moved from `regions-v9` to `regions-v10` so old candidate lists cannot mask the change.
- Known airports excluded by the coordinate boundary and airports with unusable coordinates are carried into flight and calendar coverage. The web UI reports counts and up to 100 airport codes per group; it also shows the included country set, policy source, top-three exclusions, airport-limit omissions and unsearched pairs. Wikidata batches remain bounded at 10 countries and 5,000 airport rows per batch; a saturated batch reports unknown additional airport identities. This convention is explicit and approximate, not a claim that airports straddling borders can be split or that the list is exhaustive.
- Added regressions proving Türkiye is outside the M49 Europe country set, western Russia airports can be included while airports beyond the longitude boundary are omitted, top-three popularity exclusions still apply, and calendar coverage preserves geographic omissions. Focused geography/calendar/provider tests passed 60/60. Added `npm run check:region-coverage`; rebuilt-site Chromium verified the M49 scope, top-three exclusion, boundary/unknown-coordinate disclosures and unsearched-airport disclosure with provider/job calls intercepted. Existing route-settings, calendar-ranges and search-settings browser checks also passed.
- Read-only live `resolveRegion("Europe")` at 2026-11-06T22:01:45Z used an OurAirports snapshot from 19:20Z and a fresh bounded Wikidata snapshot. It resolved 51 country/area entries, returned 100 selected airports and 743 known candidates beyond the resolver selection, excluded LHR/CDG/MAD, and began at FRA. It reported 268 known airports outside the approximate boundary and none without coordinates; the UI lists up to 100 codes per omission class. No fare search was started.
- Final verification: full `npm test` passed 414/414; `npm run build` passed; `npm audit --audit-level=low` found 0 vulnerabilities. Rebuilt-loopback Chromium checks passed for region coverage, route settings, calendar ranges, search settings, carrier guidance, hotel room handoff and default flight behavior. Local app returned HTTP 200; `/api/health` at 22:07:01.936Z reported `ok`, queue depth 0, one live worker, three stale historical worker rows, one stopped worker, and zero expired leases/deadlines. No fare search was made for this change.
- Other requested region definitions remain partial: provisional Scandinavia/Mediterranean/Oceania/Pacific-Islands meanings, overlapping custom regions, other transcontinental boundaries, country-to-airport omissions, and live region fare coverage still need validation and evidence. No region claims exhaustive worldwide coverage.

## Booking.com Demand API response path re-audited — 2026-11-06

This earlier audit note was superseded by the integration recorded at the top of this handoff. At this checkpoint the response normalizer and worker/UI connection had not yet been implemented.

- At that earlier checkpoint, `lib/booking.mjs` submitted exact guest ages and per-room allocations but intentionally returned no offers, and the worker/UI had not yet been wired. The current state and verification are recorded in the top section above.
- Current official Demand API docs confirm search can return current matching property availability, prices, product-level room allocation/policies, charge breakdowns and Booking.com URLs when the appropriate `extras` are requested. Property name, address/location and class require a separate static `/accommodations/details` request; details alone do not establish price or availability. Search response and property detail IDs must be joined and validated before showing a result. Search coverage may differ from Booking.com’s consumer site.
- Exact next implementation steps: resolve and submit the selected Booking.com city ID (rather than infer a destination from the airport radius); request bounded search rows with `products` and `extra_charges`; bulk-fetch details for returned IDs (API supports up to 100 IDs/request); join only exact IDs; validate city, class, every room’s guest allocation, currency, product totals, charge inclusion and safe Booking.com URLs; surface the adapter in the durable worker and UI only when partner credentials exist. Then obtain authorized partner credentials for a live read-only query and verify returned products against the UI. Credentials are not configured, so no provider request or live occupancy/price claim was made.
- References: [Search v3.3](https://developers.booking.com/demand/docs/open-api/3.3/demand-api/accommodations/accommodations/search), [Details v3.3](https://developers.booking.com/demand/docs/open-api/3.3/demand-api/accommodations/accommodations/details), [search response examples](https://developers.booking.com/demand/docs/accommodations/search-examples), [pricing guide](https://developers.booking.com/demand/docs/accommodations/prices-accommodations). No booking endpoint or purchase flow is part of this work.

## Local worker health endpoint added — 2026-11-06

- Added loopback-only `GET /api/health`, backed by aggregate SQLite job counts and worker heartbeat rows. It exposes queue depth by product/status, oldest queued time, live/stale/stopped worker counts, expired lease/deadline counts, and observation time. It returns no job IDs, user queries, results, tokens, provider payloads or credentials; it is not an authenticated public deployment health service.
- The worker writes a heartbeat on each poll and marks itself stopped on orderly shutdown. A running worker is considered live only when its heartbeat is no more than five seconds old. Health reports `degraded` when queued work has no live worker or expired work is present.
- Regression tests cover healthy and degraded snapshots, private-data omission, and cross-origin/non-local refusal. Live loopback probe at `2026-11-06T19:48:28.022Z` returned HTTP 200, `status: ok`, queue depth 0, one live worker, zero stale workers, zero expired leases and zero expired deadlines.
- Full `npm test` passed 404/404; `npm run build` passed and `npm audit --audit-level=low` found 0 vulnerabilities. After restart, Chromium `check:search-settings` and `check:calendar-source` also passed against the built site with provider calls intercepted. Website and worker remain local-only at `127.0.0.1:3000`.
- P12 remains partial: aggregate local health is not alerting, user authentication, general abuse quota/rate limiting, credential encryption/rotation, backup/restore verification, or a production deployment security review. Do not expose the app publicly until those gates pass.

## Job ownership and current hotel-source evidence — 2026-11-06

- Durable jobs now require a per-job UUID v4 owner capability for enqueue, status, cancel, pause, resume, human browser input and deletion. The SQLite database stores only its SHA-256 digest; owner checks use constant-time comparison. Seller-link lookup also restricts the selected Ignav offer to that owner. The browser keeps the capability in that tab’s `sessionStorage` next to the job ID; reload restoration reuses it. Old ID-only tab state is discarded, and legacy active database rows without an owner digest are cancelled during schema migration.
- This is job ownership for the local single-user app, not user authentication. It does not provide accounts, session revocation, per-user quotas, general rate limiting, monitoring, or protection from same-origin script compromise. Other non-job provider endpoints remain local-origin guarded. Keep the listener loopback-only until public deployment controls are designed and validated.
- Verification before this latest Europe update: `npm test` passed 392/392; `npm run build` passed; `npm audit` found 0 vulnerabilities. Built-loopback `check:search-settings`, `check:calendar-source`, `check:calendar-ranges`, and `check:flight-default` passed with job/provider boundaries intercepted. The settings browser check verified the owner capability on start, status poll and reload restoration. The latest 394-test/build/audit results are above.

Its hosted page returned property JSON-LD with a Key West address and a stated two-adult/no-child occupancy, but the public booking UI fell back to “page is currently not loading”; its first-party `/booking/property_info` request returned HTTP 403. ## Skyscanner exact-calendar source — 2026-10-31

- The exact fare calendar now lets users select Ignav or Skyscanner. Skyscanner queries use the existing adapter inside the same durable, checkpointed fare session and request budget; returned offers must have a matching source reference, validated route/date/segments, CAD total, fresh quote and complete provider search. Incomplete provider polling yields no “lowest” date price. Calendar cells link to the returned provider handoff and do not present an Ignav seller lookup for Skyscanner offers.

The test suite verifies the Skyscanner calendar path and confirms a missing key fails without a network request. No live Skyscanner fare was retrieved because no partner key was configured.

## Skyscanner round-trip and polling continuation — 2026-10-31

- Implemented native two-direction Skyscanner requests through the existing flight provider router and refresh path. Both returned directions must match the actual airport/date query and contain every referenced segment; missing dates/times and reversed destination-stay chronology are excluded. Contract tests use synthetic offline transports, not production fare fallbacks. No credentialed supplier request or booking was made; live search and seller acceptance remain unverified pending partner access.

No live supplier or new visual browser check was run. The full `work.md` goal remains incomplete.

## Audit correctness fixes — 2026-10-31

- Applied the eight reproduced audit fixes after explicit user authorization. Omkar and Matrix money parsers reject invalid comma grouping and unsafe amounts; Omkar also rejects signed/partial prices and conflicting currency or per-fare traveler scope. Unsupported passenger aliases (including `infantsInLap`) are rejected by shared validation before supplier requests rather than silently omitted.
- Fully normalized Matrix itineraries now require returned cabin evidence for every flight in each direction. Contradictory cabins are rejected; missing cabin evidence remains a clearly labeled price-only observation. Existing price-only display, adult-only limitations and no-booking behavior are preserved.
- Connection filters use segment airport evidence when present, not an inconsistent `via` summary. The flight schema rejects contradictory summaries. The exact calendar derives its canonical connection summary before validation and retains its country-filter regression. Omkar segment airport fields now contain IATA codes, not display labels.
- Matrix's native-fetch path now participates in shared SQLite source pacing/cooldowns, including key discovery. HTTP-200 multipart access/rate refusals also persist a cooldown and return retry time. The separate-ticket source-refusal branch uses the validated request limit instead of an undefined variable.
- Unified results honor the submitted query's price, descending-price, duration or departure sort. Price-only entries lack verified duration/departure and appear last for those time-based sorts. No new runtime dependency, provider request, account action or booking was introduced.
- Verification: all 287 tests passed (`npm test -- --test-concurrency=2`), production build passed, and `npm audit` found zero vulnerabilities. The first full run caught an interaction with the calendar's existing connection-summary canonicalization; it was corrected in shared validation/canonicalization and the final full run passed. No new live-provider or visual browser QA was performed. Broader source availability, seller confirmation and the package-widget initialization blocker described below remain unresolved; these correctness fixes do not complete `work.md`.

## Identified segment-date guard follow-up — 2026-10-31

Current code review found a fare-category gap: identified travelers were checked against all returned local segment dates, while age-only count queries were checked only against the requested departure and return dates. An age-11 child on an overnight outbound could therefore receive an offer even though Ignav classifies age 12+ as adult and the segment arrival date was unknown from the count query.

`lib/ignav.mjs` now runs the age-only round-trip boundary guard across every observed departure and arrival date on the outbound and inbound segments. Offers that may cross age 12 without a DOB are withheld; the result limitations disclose this. Added a regression that first failed for an age-11 overnight arrival, confirms the offer is withheld, and confirms age 10 and same-day age-11 travel remain eligible for normalization. Full suite: 279 passed; production build passed; `npm audit` found zero vulnerabilities. These are fixture tests; no fare request or booking was made for this change.

The same-day/overnight rule is an eligibility-category safeguard, not proof of airline fare eligibility. Long-trip age-only parties are conservatively rejected by the earlier duration rule. Carrier fare basis, operating-carrier requirements, and seller acceptance remain open. Priority 2 remains partial.

Package status: the full UI runner again failed before package submission during origin lookup. Earlier direct live checks show the widget sometimes overwrites typed text with its Toronto default. Code now checks typed text and retries once. That has not yielded a reliable full live package result. Continue from this blocker only after inspecting the current widget request/input timing; do not repeat a package search until both exact location selections succeed.

## Booking.com multi-room child search guard — 2026-10-30

- Auditing priority 4 against the actual hotel UI and worker exposed a gap: the Booking.com browser adapter rejected multi-room child searches only when room allocations were already present. Aggregate adult/child/room counts without allocations could therefore reach a browser search that did not prove which child was assigned to which room.
- `validateStay` now rejects a multi-room child query without exact allocations, which makes `POST /api/search/jobs` return HTTP 400 before creating a durable job. When exact allocations are present, the Booking.com browser adapter refuses the multi-room child request before source/browser access because its current consumer-page parser cannot verify room-level occupancy. It does not silently fall back to aggregate counts.

Current code now has a distinct wired path, while credentials and live inventory remain absent.
- Added a regression at the adapter layer and the durable-job API route. The first full-suite run found one existing fixture that now needed explicit room allocations; the fixture was corrected to name adult ages and assign each child to a room. Focused verification: 33 hotel/source tests and 14 route/challenge tests passed. Final verification: `npm test -- --test-concurrency=2` passed 268 tests; `npm run build` passed; `npm audit` reported zero vulnerabilities. No Booking.com request was made.
- Priority 4 remains partial. Next: wire an authorized partner API path into the hotel product only after response fields can prove property identity, stars, per-room product/occupancy, dates, CAD price, fees, cancellation and booking URL; otherwise preserve the current fail-closed behavior. Verify actual child-party property inventory, not only request serialization.

3. **Alternatives and splits:** bounded alternate pairs, exact flight/UTC matching, contiguous ticket blocks, round trips, identity partitions, quote freshness and budgets are tested. No live cross-airport/split fare comparison, simultaneous seat inventory or transfer/baggage/entry costs are proven.

Child parties require real ages; no child live run, checkout refresh, exact property, local charges or seller confirmation exists.

The fresh website rerun returned 29 CAD price-only observations for YHZ–LAX, but zero full itineraries passed validation. 8. **Geographic regions:** versioned policies exist for all 28 requested labels. Middle East and Levant & Beyond now use explicit Travel Hunter country sets rather than failing closed; several slash/custom scopes remain provisional. Europe popularity resolution excludes the three highest recorded-traffic airports and starts at rank four, returning 100 candidates with more available. Region airport sets remain bounded and some transcontinental membership is country-level; no fares were searched in that Europe ranking run.
9. **Agency/provider access:** Omkar, NDC and other adapters are limited to documented/configured scopes; no production GDS entitlement or current live agency itinerary is evidenced. Directory links and mocks are not inventory.
10. **Official hotel engines:** Reservit remains the tested direct engine. No second discovered official booking engine with verified live CAD offers is established.
11. **Fare calendars:** bounded multi-airport/region pair-by-date Ignav calendars preserve query constraints and disclose unfinished pairs; live regional coverage and more sources remain open.
12. **Operations/security:** local-origin gates, streaming JSON caps, durable jobs, per-job owner capabilities, worker recovery tests and a loopback aggregate health endpoint exist. User authentication/accounts, general abuse quotas, alerting, recovery validation, deployment review and live provider-outage evidence remain incomplete. Keep local-only.
13. **Themes and controls:** dark/light preference, source/party/route/price/duration/departure/layover/airline settings, and bounded calendar/comparison/package settings are exposed. Airline name/code suggestions use Wikidata and clearly disclose incomplete/historical data; provider live filter-impact proof remains open.

### Latest code change and reviewable files

The latest implementation change closes an unsafe multi-room Booking.com child-search path. Before the fix, a query with multiple rooms and child ages could proceed without room assignments because the browser adapter only refused a multi-room query when allocation data already existed. The current behavior is deliberately fail-closed:

- `lib/booking-browser.mjs` requires exact room allocations for multiple-room child parties and rejects this browser adapter's multi-room child searches before opening a provider page, including when allocations are supplied. The adapter cannot prove child-to-room placement from its current consumer-page flow.
- `lib/hotel-search.mjs` validates the stay and rejects missing allocations before queuing; `POST /api/search/jobs` therefore returns 400 for this invalid shape.
- Tests cover the adapter rejection, the no-browser/no-provider-call behavior, and the durable API rejection. One existing challenge fixture was corrected to supply real adult ages and exact child room assignments.
- The official-hotel adapter still accepts the validated allocation form. The separate Demand API adapter serializes the per-room request contract under tests, but remains disconnected from the hotel UI, has no configured partner credentials, and intentionally creates no displayed offers without verifiable response fields.
- This is a correctness guard, not multi-room Booking.com support. Keep Priority 4 partial until real, authorized property/room/occupancy/price/fee evidence is normalized and shown end to end.

The package-search follow-up changed exact location selection to search using the selected Air Canada Vacations provider label, rather than typing an IATA code and waiting for a full-label result. `lib/vacation-form.mjs`, `lib/vacation-search.mjs`, and `lib/vacation-suggestions.mjs` contain that path; tests cover the label contract. The direct live adapter observed 20 results, but the latest local UI rerun stopped in provider suggestion selection. Do not present the direct adapter result as a fresh UI/worker rendering proof.

### Evidence distinctions for the next session

- **Live adapter observation:** a provider response was retrieved and normalized, but this does not prove that the website rendered it or that checkout will honor it.
- **Website/worker result:** the local UI submitted a real query through the configured worker and rendered provider observations; this still does not prove seller availability or final price.
- **Price-only Matrix observation:** Matrix returned a price observation with no itinerary that passed the app's full validation. It is not a fully identified flight, seller quote, or bookable offer.
- **Fixture/browser check:** verifies local rendering and behavior only; it is not live inventory evidence.
- **Seller-verified:** requires a current seller lookup that matches all itinerary segments and exposes current availability, baggage and mandatory charges. No such current proof exists in this audit.
- **Booked:** not applicable; purchases and reservations are prohibited for this work.

Keep `IMPLEMENTATION-STATUS.md`'s §17 table aligned with these distinctions. For geographic searches, preserve the exact selected/expanded airports, searched pairs, unsearched pairs, resolver caps, and source refusal. For every comparison, retain query constraints, party assignments, quote time/currency, and budget usage. Never describe a bounded sample as exhaustive market coverage or simultaneous inventory.

## Booking.com Demand API room allocations — 2026-10-30

- `validateHotelSearch` now accepts exact multi-room allocations using the existing `adultAges`/`childrenAges` room shape. It verifies room count, one or more actual adults per room, each age range, total adult count, and that the allocated child-age multiset exactly matches the entered party. A multi-room child query without allocations is rejected before network access.
- The configured Booking.com Demand API request now serializes `guests.allocation` with each room’s adult count and child ages, while preserving the aggregate `number_of_adults`, `number_of_rooms`, and `children`. This follows the official [Demand API Search schema](https://developers.booking.com/demand/docs/open-api/3.3/demand-api/accommodations/accommodations/search) and its [occupancy/allocation examples](https://developers.booking.com/demand/docs/accommodations/occupancy-use-cases).
- Added mock-transport regressions for exact request shape, missing allocations, mismatched ages, and rooms without adults. `node --test tests/booking.test.mjs` passed 5 tests. Full verification: 266 tests passed, `npm run build` passed, and `npm audit` found zero vulnerabilities.
- At this contract-only checkpoint, no Booking.com Demand API token or affiliate ID was configured, so no live search was made. The adapter marked successful raw search data `incomplete` and returned no offers until property rating, occupancy details, room products, link, fees, and price evidence were verified. The separate request-contract improvement did not connect to the website UI. The current browser-path guard is documented in the newer “Booking.com multi-room child search guard” section above; no Booking.com multi-room result is shown in the UI. Priority 4 remains partial.

## Bounded API JSON request bodies — 2026-10-30

- Replaced direct JSON/body buffering in all API routes and the shared comparison endpoint with [`readJsonRequest`](lib/request-json.mjs). It checks a valid `Content-Length` before opening the reader, enforces route-specific byte caps while streaming, cancels over-limit streams, and returns HTTP 413. Flight/search, calendar, package, hotel, and comparison submissions use 12,000 bytes; assistant/provider refresh use 4,000; airport/seller lookups use 2,000; member/package-location lookups use 1,000.
- Existing calendar and comparison route tests now assert HTTP 413 for oversized input. New helper tests cover exact-limit acceptance, chunked overflow/cancellation, and early Content-Length rejection. Focused tests passed 17; `rg` confirms no direct `request.json()`/`request.text()` parsing remains in `app/api` or `lib/comparison-endpoint.mjs`.

## Legacy search API local-only gate — 2026-10-30

- Audited the API route files and their shared handlers. `/api/search/optimize` and `/api/search/separate-tickets` delegate to `queueComparison`, which enforces `localRequest`; the other search, seller, hotel, package, member, airport, and assistant routes have local-origin guards.
- Found `/api/search` was an exception: requests with `source: "google"` bypassed the local-origin guard, and the route parsed JSON before checking origin. Removed that exception and moved the guard before parsing. The endpoint now responds 403 for a remote Google request and for malformed remote JSON without entering provider execution.
- Added `tests/search-route.test.mjs` for remote Google rejection, guard-before-parse behavior, and same-origin malformed-body handling. Focused route checks passed 3 tests. Full verification after the change: 261 tests passed, `npm run build` passed, and `npm audit` found zero vulnerabilities.
- This keeps all current APIs behind the local-origin check; it is not authentication or user/job ownership, and request-rate limits, operational monitoring and other public deployment gates remain open. No provider request was sent.

## Porter child and youth guidance — 2026-10-30

- Added Porter Airlines (`PD`) to the source-linked advisory shown in flight offer details. It recognizes explicit operating/marketing codes and the supported airline-name fallback, computes supplied birth-date ages across the known query and segment dates, and reports age-2/8/12 thresholds without treating general guidance as fare eligibility.

The advisory remains unverified for a particular fare, operating carrier, route, codeshare, age/flight circumstance, and seller; it does not authorize a booking partition or establish a price.
- Added regressions for a child turning 8 mid-trip and the Porter infant minimum age: a supplied DOB under 7 days at travel is flagged, while exactly 7 days is not flagged as below the minimum. `node --test tests/carrier-eligibility.test.mjs` passes 12 tests. Priority 2 remains partial.

## Geographic convention audit — 2026-10-30

- Compared all 28 `work.md` §4 labels with the current UNSD M49 taxonomy. The detailed per-label baseline and remaining ambiguities are in [geographic-conventions-audit.md](docs/geographic-conventions-audit.md).
- M49 provides a defensible baseline for many continent/subregion labels and explicitly distinguishes North America from Northern America. It assigns each area to one region, so requested overlapping/custom scopes need unions. Documented territory and transcontinental cautions include Greenland vs Denmark, Saint Pierre and Miquelon vs France, Turkey/Istanbul, and Egypt/Middle East.
- The current Wikidata resolver still resolves one exact entity and uses country membership; it does not implement these composite scopes. It caps output at 300 country areas and 10 airports, with a selected 3/5/10-airport request limit and bounded pairs. No fares were searched for the 28 regions as part of this review.
- Product scope remains unresolved for Northern Europe vs Scandinavia, Southern Europe vs Mediterranean, Middle East, Levant & Beyond, GCC vs broader Gulf, Oceania vs South Pacific, and geographic vs institutional Pacific Islands. Keep these partial and do not claim all-region coverage until product boundaries and live airport/pair coverage are validated.

## Exact ages on observed flight dates — 2026-10-30

- Carrier guidance now evaluates supplied birth dates against the query dates and every valid local departure/arrival date in the observed outbound and return segments. A birthday on an overnight or later segment can therefore surface the relevant published age boundary even when it falls after the form’s departure date.
- Leap-day ages now fail closed on both February 28 and March 1 in non-leap years until the carrier's convention is verified. The prior calculation rejected February 28 but silently assigned the March 1 convention. A regression confirms an ambiguous March 1 DOB is rejected before Ignav receives a fare request.
- Added a regression where an American Airlines itinerary departs on the child’s fifth birthday, one day after the requested departure date; the old query-date-only logic missed the age-4-to-5 transition. The focused carrier suite passes 10 tests; at this milestone the full suite passed 247 tests. The current full suite passes 266 tests; `npm run build` passes; `npm audit` reports zero vulnerabilities.
- Controlled Chromium confirmed the identified traveler and guardian reached the local fixture job, then displayed the AA age-4-to-5 boundary when the observed arrival date was the child’s fifth birthday. All travel responses were fixtures, external traffic was blocked, and the page had zero errors. No live fare was queried.
- The guidance remains advisory and **unverified** for the actual fare, route, operating carrier, codeshare and seller. Missing segment dates do not get inferred, and this does not authorize or confirm a booking partition. Priority 2 remains partial.
- Next: add authoritative, dated carrier rules only for the route/cabin conditions those sources cover, then validate identified passenger groups against every operating segment. Keep unknown carriers, fare-basis eligibility, and seller acceptance unverified until there is source evidence.

For Air Canada-marketed or explicitly Air Canada-operated segments, it links to Air Canada's published child/infant fare guidance and explains that route type affects child fares, under-12 and 12+ categories differ, and infant pricing/seating varies. - The advisory always reports eligibility as **unverified**. It does not apply a discount or convert a returned fare into an eligibility claim. With exact birth dates it calls out a travel-date change across the under-12/12+ boundary; count-only ages and unsupported carriers remain explicit unknowns.
- Four focused regression tests passed. The full suite passed 226 tests (`npm test -- --test-concurrency=2`), `npm run build` passed, and `npm audit` found zero vulnerabilities. Controlled Chromium rendered the advisory from a test-only intercepted result with zero page errors. No live fare, seller, or carrier eligibility was verified. The build still warns that Next ignores `/home/rayyan/package-lock.json` because the project is nested below `/home/rayyan`.
- Priority 2 remains partial: this is general Air Canada guidance, not a complete carrier/fare rules engine; exact operating carriers, fare basis rules, codeshares, infant ratios, and live seller acceptance still require verification.

## Regional taxonomy review — 2026-10-30

- Compared `work.md` §4's 28 requested labels with the official UN Statistics Division M49 geography. M49 is a useful baseline for standard region/subregion membership, but it places each country or area in one region only. The requested product labels include overlapping/custom groupings (for example “North America” alongside “The Caribbean,” “Europe” alongside its subregions, “Greenland & St. Pierre,” “Gulf States,” and “Levant & Beyond”), so M49 alone cannot define all 28 search meanings. The UN also distinguishes the broad North America grouping from “Northern America.” [UNSD M49 geographic regions](https://unstats.un.org/unsd/methodology/m49)
- No search taxonomy was changed in this review. The current resolver still uses bounded Wikidata country/airport relationships and explicitly warns that transcontinental membership may include out-of-scope airports. The exact custom country/territory sets and Turkey/Egypt airport treatment remain unresolved; do not describe the existing returned country list as validated against all 28 product labels.
