## Exact age-boundary continuation — 2026-10-30

- Ignav accepts children ages 2–11 and classifies age 12+ as adult ([one-way request schema](https://ignav.com/docs/one-way)). The count-only form provides only age at departure, so an 11-year-old on a later-return round trip could enter a different provider category without the user's birth date. The shared validator now rejects that request before enqueue; users are directed to identified-traveler mode. Identified travelers continue through per-date age validation.
- Tests cover no provider fare request for the age-11 case, acceptance of age 10, and the actual local flight-job endpoint rejecting age 11 before enqueue. Controlled Chromium submitted the form, observed HTTP 400, rendered the explanation, and recorded zero page errors. `npm test -- --test-concurrency=2`: 222 passed; `npm run build` passed with the existing home lockfile warning; `npm audit` found zero vulnerabilities. No fare request was made for this browser check.
- This closes one provider-category boundary, not carrier/fare eligibility. Exact child/youth discounts, operating-carrier and codeshare rules, infant ratios by airline, and all other suppliers remain unverified; keep unknowns explicit and preserve guardian assignment for every partition.

## Persisted-offer seller match continuation — 2026-10-30

- The local seller-links endpoint now retrieves the original Ignav offer from completed flight, calendar or comparison job results and compares each flight segment (carrier, flight number, airports, and UTC schedule) with the booking-links response. It compares inbound segments too. If the itinerary differs, the endpoint suppresses the returned seller links and reports a mismatch; if the original offer is unavailable or either side lacks enough segment evidence, the match remains unknown. Durable result lookup is bounded to 100 recent matching job rows.

A route integration test persists an offer, stubs Ignav HTTP, calls the actual local endpoint, and verifies the exact match plus complete one-way leg coverage. Store tests cover retrieving offers from completed results. No credentials or live provider requests were used.

Both flows had zero page errors. Final checks: `npm test -- --test-concurrency=2` passed 219 tests, `npm run build` passed with the existing home lockfile warning, and `npm audit` found zero vulnerabilities. Still open: seller-reported availability, final seller price, baggage and mandatory charges. No paid/live lookup is made while remaining quota is unknown.

## Audit continuation — seller-link evidence and limits

- Current Ignav implementation (`lib/ignav.mjs`, `app/BookingOptions.js`) exposes booking links and optional CAD prices. Review against the provider's current primary docs: [booking-links](https://ignav.com/docs/booking-links), [response format](https://ignav.com/docs/response-format), [authentication and limits](https://ignav.com/docs/authentication), and [pricing](https://ignav.com/pricing).
- Ignav documents `ignav_id` as the handoff token for the selected itinerary. Link `specificity` may be `exact_flight` or `search_results`; neither establishes current availability. Link prices marked `verified` mean Ignav says the price came from a displayed fare or booking-price check; this is not a seller checkout or ticketing confirmation. `booking_options[].legs` specifies which direction(s) a purchase covers. Round trips may require selecting one option for each direction; each link price applies only to its option's covered legs. An incomplete priced set must not be added and labeled as a complete seller price.
- Code review found the current UI lists returned links and prices but does not render covered legs, link specificity, trip coverage completeness, or the meaning and scope of the provider's `verified` flag. The adapter also drops any `observed_at` field if the API supplies one. This remains a provenance gap; a provider handoff must not be presented as a seller-verified offer.
- No live booking-link lookup was made. Ignav currently documents 1,000 free requests, then $2 per 1,000 successful requests; the local account's remaining allowance is unknown, so a lookup could incur a charge. No free-quota or spending authorization was available. No seller link was opened, no account was created, and no reservation or purchase was attempted.
- Exact next step if implementation resumes: normalize and validate provider itinerary and each option's leg coverage, retain provider observation/retrieval time when supplied, distinguish provider-reported price status from seller confirmation, and add one-way/round-trip combined/split/partial-coverage tests plus a controlled UI check. Then perform a live read-only lookup only after its authorized free allowance is known; open any returned handoff only for read-only verification. Never submit a booking or payment.

### Traveler-source audit

- Official Reservit hotel search now accepts explicit per-room adult and child ages. Server validation requires an adult in each room and verifies that allocations match the full party. Booking.com's adapter does not verify per-room allocation and does not accept these allocation requests.

No live multi-room CAD rates were verified. `npm test`: 180 passed; `npm audit`: zero vulnerabilities; final build passed with the existing lockfile warning; 15 focused hotel checks passed after the request-count UI change. Temporary QA server stopped.

The documentation does not establish a combined multi-room rate or simultaneous inventory. A different age, room count or stay cannot produce the subtotal. The displayed average uses all travelers, and fees remain explicitly unknown. This is an independent observed subtotal, not a supplier package or verified all-in total.

No live child/infant split fare or airline eligibility has been verified. Identified comparison session storage now holds a generic job reference key rather than birth dates; the validated request remains in private local job storage.

- Exact Ignav calendar now carries identified travelers, birth dates, guardians and infant seating through validated jobs and checkpointed fare requests. It evaluates each outbound/return date independently, marks a category-changing trip unsupported without making a provider request, and continues later dates. Known unsupported dates are counted separately and prevent a claim of full scan coverage. Selecting/restoring a calendar query restores the identified party to the flight form. Actual returned segment dates still pass the Ignav adapter's category check.
- Tests cover a birthday spanning the calendar, category-change refusal, preserved party, request budget and checkpoint reuse. Chromium with an intercepted job response verified the identified party and lap choice reach calendar submission; no live infant fare was requested. `npm test`: 176 passed; `npm run build`: passed with the existing home lockfile warning; `npm audit`: zero vulnerabilities. The temporary local QA server was stopped.
- This extends exact-airport Ignav calendars only. Carrier-specific eligibility, actual live infant pricing, category-changing trips, region/multi-airport calendars, guardian-safe ticket splits and birth-date retention controls remain open. Historical notes saying identified calendars are unsupported are superseded.

- Superseding the earlier statement that the identity path was not exposed: the flight form now collects birth dates, guardian assignments and infant seat/lap choices. Flight jobs and provider aggregation validate and preserve that party; Ignav receives its documented categories and infant counts, and other flight adapters report unsupported parties. Exact fare calendar, hotel add-on and adult-only ticket comparisons do not operate on identified parties yet. Ignav rejects a returned itinerary if an observed segment's local departure or arrival date changes a traveler's fare category.
- Browser QA with an intercepted queue response confirmed an adult plus lap infant submission preserved the guardian and seating, without JavaScript errors. The aggregator and adapter were checked with test transports; no live infant fare or carrier eligibility was verified. The full suite passed 175 tests, production build passed with the existing home lockfile warning, and `npm audit` found zero vulnerabilities. A temporary local QA server was stopped after the check.
- Remaining: test a real eligible infant fare and seller handoff, verify carrier rules and ages for every segment, handle birthdays that change fare category, carry identities through calendars, room allocations and guardian-safe ticket partitions, and confirm retention/deletion controls for birth dates. Job records are local private SQLite data; do not expose them publicly.

- Direct `searchIgnav` now accepts a mutually exclusive identified-traveler array, evaluates birth dates on outbound/return dates, validates explicit adult guardian and infant lap/seat assignment, and sends Ignav's documented `infants_in_seat` and `infants_on_lap` fields. A change of provider category during the trip is refused before network access. This path preserves IDs and actual dates in its query result, and explicitly leaves carrier eligibility and seller terms to verification. It does not establish eligibility on connecting flight dates or a live infant fare.
- This identity path is **not exposed through the app form, aggregator, queue, calendar, or optimizer**; those continue to reject it. The lower-level cheapest-dates helper also rejects identities rather than dropping them. Next wire a single validated party through all those paths, account for actual segment dates and guard result actions, then verify with a real permitted source. Mock transport verified the outgoing seated-infant fields and category-change refusal; no live infant search was made. `npm test`: 174 passed; build passed with existing lockfile warning; `npm audit`: zero vulnerabilities. After a final mixed-input guard, 13 focused tests passed.

- Party-integrity fix: shared validation and the direct Google, Ignav, Expedia, Skyscanner and Air Canada search adapters reject unsupported infant/identity fields instead of silently pricing an adult-only party. Tests exercise direct adapters and aggregation without making supplier requests. This is a refusal until the supported infant flow is implemented, not infant fare support. `npm test` passed 172 tests, `npm run build` passed with the existing lockfile warning, and `npm audit` found zero vulnerabilities.

- Added independent identity validation in `lib/travelers.mjs`: actual birth dates are evaluated on each supplied travel date, minors require an identified adult guardian, infants require explicit lap/seat selection, and one guardian cannot hold multiple lap infants. Leap-day age on February 28 of a non-leap year requires carrier verification. This is a conservative validation foundation, not a statement of airline eligibility.
- The new model is **not connected to the form, jobs, provider requests, calendar or optimizer yet**; those continue to reject infants and child partitions. Connect and verify the entire path before exposing infant search. `npm test` passed 171 tests, `npm run build` passed with the existing lockfile warning, and `npm audit` found zero vulnerabilities. The two new tests cover birthday boundaries, ambiguous leap-day ages, guardian assignment, infant seating and lap conflicts.

- Current `validateTravelers` accepts child ages 2–17 at departure and rejects infants; comparison jobs reject every child/youth party. Ignav maps ages 12–17 to its adult category, but the input does not establish a birth date, return-date age or carrier eligibility. Guardian relationships are not represented, so an adult count alone cannot prove a safe booking partition.
- Ignav's current [one-way API documentation](https://ignav.com/docs/one-way) documents separate `infants_in_seat` and `infants_on_lap` counts, and specifies that lap infants cannot exceed adult-category passengers. [Expedia's Flight Listings documentation](https://developers.expediagroup.com/xap-apis/api/shopping-apis/flight-listings) documents `infantInLap` and `infantInSeat` as well as exact child ages. These source parameters establish an implementation path, not individual airline eligibility or guardian assignment.
- Next implement an explicit traveler identity/birth-date and infant seating model with guardian links, validate age on each travel date, carry it through the form, job persistence and supported provider request paths, and keep unsupported or unverified carrier rules visible. Then extend comparisons only for assignments whose guardian and provider eligibility are evidenced. Calendar dates and return legs require separate age evaluation; do not reuse an outbound age across dates without checking it.

## API surface

- `POST /api/search` searches `google`, `ignav`, `expedia`, `expedia-omkar`, `skyscanner`, `air-canada`, `ita-matrix`, or `source: "all"`; unavailable providers are reported without substituting results.
- `POST /api/search/refresh` refreshes Skyscanner or Air Canada NDC offers, or retrieves Ignav seller options. Expedia Travel Redirect does not have a refresh operation wired here.
- `POST /api/search/booking-links` gets Ignav seller links. Expedia and Skyscanner return provider handoff URLs with search results.
- Credentialed endpoints are restricted to same-origin localhost requests. Credentials must remain in server-side `.env.local`, never browser code or source control.

## Providers

| Provider | Adapter status | Required configuration / limitation |
|---|---|---|
| Google Flights | Existing api-anything read operations (`search`, `top`) | User states written permission. One adult, one-way, economy; search result is not a seller-confirmed fare. |
| Ignav | REST search, booking-link lookup, and split-party optimizer | `IGNAV_API_KEY`. Configured locally. A live YHZ–BCN search for 2027-04-15 on 2026-10-29 UTC returned 12 accepted CAD itineraries; seller links and booking remain unverified. |
| Expedia Travel Redirect | Documented Flight Listings one-way/round-trip group search, Expedia deeplink and exact-flight-only re-query refresh | `EXPEDIA_TRAVEL_REDIRECT_KEY`, `EXPEDIA_TRAVEL_REDIRECT_AUTHORIZATION`; partner onboarding required. Child searches require actual ages; flexible child calendars need DOB support and remain excluded. No live Expedia quote is verified. |
| Expedia via Omkar | Documented Omkar one-way API adapter and Expedia search-link builder | `OMKAR_API_KEY`; one adult/one-way only. Requires explicit CAD currency and one-traveler scope. Selenium round-trip automation is not enabled. |
| Expedia via Omkar | Documented one-way endpoint, fare-tier links, price-only fallback for incomplete schedule details, and Expedia search URL builder | `OMKAR_API_KEY`; one adult/one-way only. CAD and one-traveler scope must be explicit. No live call, no cached prices, no round-trip pricing. |
| Skyscanner | Live Prices create/poll, deep links, and itinerary refresh | `SKYSCANNER_API_KEY`; partner access required. Child ages must be supplied. Refresh is not a checkout guarantee. |
| Air Canada NDC | NDC 17.2 `AirShopping` and `OfferPrice` SOAP/XML | `AIR_CANADA_NDC_API_KEY`, `AIR_CANADA_NDC_SELLER_ID`, `AIR_CANADA_NDC_IATA_NUMBER`, `AIR_CANADA_NDC_AGENCY_ID`; optional `AIR_CANADA_NDC_BASE_URL`. Approved seller onboarding required. Mock-tested only; first adapter supports one adult. No booking/payment flow. |

Copy `.env.example` to `.env.local` if needed, then fill credentials obtained directly through each provider's approved onboarding. Do not send secrets in chat. Restart the Next.js server after changes.

The opt-in adult-only separate-ticket comparison supports one-way chains of two–four segments and round-trip chains of one–four segments per direction, with optional contiguous groupings and a minimum three-hour self-transfer buffer.

## Flight + hotel continuation

- Round-trip adult-only flight cards now offer + Hotel, carrying destination, dates and adult count into the existing Booking.com form.
- Matching stay observations can show an independently quoted combined subtotal and per-adult subtotal. Missing taxes remain explicitly unknown; this is not a supplier package or confirmed savings.
- Hotel dates are editable and must be reviewed against actual arrival and departure. Combined subtotal is withheld when dates or occupancy differ from the selected flight query.
- Live Booking.com extraction and complete flight/hotel availability remain unverified; full work.md scope remains incomplete.

### Flight + hotel date and amount verification

- Supersedes prior date note: hotel defaults and comparison eligibility now use observed local outbound arrival and inbound departure dates, so overnight flights do not add a spurious hotel night.
- `lib/flight-hotel.mjs` centralizes strict CAD amount parsing and compatibility checks. Invalid amounts, currency/occupancy/date mismatches, and unsupported parties yield no combined subtotal.
- Added meaningful regression tests; full suite passes 52 tests. This does not prove live Booking.com availability, inclusive taxes, or package bookability.

### Hotel star filter

- Booking.com browser searches accept a validated minimum of four or five stars. The form offers 4–5 stars or 5 stars only.
- The provider URL applies the selected class filter; extraction independently rejects properties below the requested class. Added a regression case with mixed four/five-star cards.

### Hotel occupancy extension

- Booking.com browser form accepts 1–9 rooms and explicit comma-separated child ages (0–17); requests and sanitized hotel handoff links preserve them.
- Browser import checks the final provider URL for rooms, child count and exact ages. Child/multi-room cards must also state matching occupancy; unknown values are withheld.
- Combined flight/hotel subtotal currently rejects child hotel occupancy or multiple rooms; full-party combined pricing and property-specific room allocations remain incomplete. Live child/multi-room provider verification is pending.

### Hotel observation details

- Booking.com extraction now retains an explicitly observed cancellation-policy field; absence remains null and UI labels cancellation/refund terms unavailable. No free-cancellation assumption is applied.
- Every accepted hotel observation now includes structured stay dates, nights, room count, adult count and actual child ages, displayed alongside the original provider price/tax text.
- Regression tests cover observed non-refundable versus absent terms and exact stay metadata. Full suite: 55 passing tests. Live selector availability remains unverified and can legitimately yield unknown terms.

### Latest live hotel probe

- Live Barcelona stay query (2027-04-15–20, one adult) reached a running browser job but produced neither visible property-card observations nor a recognized challenge during 50 seconds of polling after navigation. Probe cancelled cleanly; no verified hotel price was obtained.
- Browser navigation errors are now surfaced rather than discarded, except when actual result cards or a recognized challenge are already available. Targeted browser/extraction tests: 6 pass; production build passes.
- Hotel live availability remains unresolved. This evidence does not establish that the provider had no hotels or that a CAPTCHA was present.

### Hotel session lifecycle

- Switching the selected flight or unmounting hotel search now sends cancellation for its active browser job.
- Responses from the previous selection are ignored; if an in-flight start returns an active job after selection changes, that job is cancelled too. This prevents stale hotel results from attaching to a different flight and reduces orphan browser searches.
- Cancellation is best-effort during page teardown; the server's five-minute job timeout remains the fallback. Cross-reload durable recovery is still unfinished.

### Provenance and scope audit

- Added expandable flight details for source versus seller, observation/expiry, group and average per-traveler prices, cabin, ages, booking status, fee limitations and actual segment fields. Missing metadata remains explicitly unknown.
- IMPLEMENTATION-STATUS.md maps major requirements to current files/evidence and remaining work; it explicitly preserves vacation packages, official hotels, membership rates, agency sources, complete deep optimization and production acceptance scope.

### Multi-segment separate-ticket comparisons

- Expanded adult-only one-way independent-segment pricing from exactly two to two–four observed segments. Every self-transfer boundary must use the same airport and meet the selected minimum three-hour buffer.
- Search-cost disclosure now scales with actual segments and optional adult group sizes (maximum 37 calls: one baseline plus four segments × nine group sizes).
- All segment totals and connection durations are retained. Added a three-segment comparison regression and a short later-connection rejection. Five targeted optimizer tests pass.
- Still unfinished: contiguous multi-segment ticket groupings, alternate route discovery, round-trip combinations, child/guardian eligibility and confirmed all-in savings. This is not exhaustive route optimization.

### Contiguous ticket groupings

- Opt-in contiguous grouping now enumerates all eligible partitions of the observed two–four-segment chain into at least two tickets. Every returned block must match the exact original flights and schedules; unknown/missing block prices produce no plan.
- The self-transfer buffer applies only at ticket boundaries. Connections inside a provider-observed through ticket may be shorter. Adult booking allocations can be optimized independently for each block.
- Searches are deduplicated by contiguous block and party size; UI reports upper bound (up to 82 calls for four segments and nine adult group sizes). Results rank observed totals; mandatory extras and seat availability remain unverified.
- Six targeted optimizer regressions pass, including a short protected internal connection with a valid later ticket boundary. Live grouping verification, alternate routes, round trips and child eligibility remain pending.

### Optimizer coverage verification

- Separate-ticket responses and UI now show actual search count, eligible ticket partitions, distinct/priced blocks and complete plans. Missing block observations exclude only affected plans, with partial coverage exposed.
- Four-segment regression verifies all seven multi-ticket partitions using nine deduplicated block queries plus the baseline. A missing-block regression verifies partial coverage without inventing prices. Eight targeted optimizer tests pass; production build passes.
- Baseline comparison now also requires a finite positive CAD price. All coverage remains restricted to the original observed flight chain, not all possible routes or source inventory.

Exact suggestion-label entry is deliberately basic; a verified package-specific suggestion picker remains needed. Durable/cancellable jobs and full `work.md` scope remain unfinished.

- A new live adapter run for YHZ–Barcelona, April 15–25, 2027 failed waiting for `#flight_hotel_tab-from-input`; no fare was imported.
- A separate rendered-page inspection after 12 seconds showed the real Flight & Hotel landing page but no booking widget/location inputs, only marketing text and unrelated inputs. This does not establish a CAPTCHA or lack of inventory. Earlier successful widget probes are not sufficient proof for the current page state.

### Follow-up widget failure diagnosis — 2026-10-29

- Reproduced on the Flight & Hotel landing page after 12 seconds: `#flight_hotel_tab-from-input` count remained zero.
- Captured failed resource requests. A first-party `GET /session-authentication/token/jwt` to `vacations.aircanada.com` failed with `net::ERR_CONNECTION_CLOSED`; several first-party images and static assets failed similarly. Console reported `getUserToken() : Failed to fetch`. Marketing page itself rendered.
- This is evidence of transient first-party network/session-auth resource failure in this browser environment, not absence of the package widget, a CAPTCHA, or no inventory. The app route currently waits on the missing input and then fails; it should return a clear provider temporarily unavailable error with diagnostics that omit full URLs/tokens. Retry strategy should be bounded and only retry navigation/widget loading, not duplicate a submitted package search.
- No code change resolves the upstream connection failure in this environment. Earlier successful form and live listing probes remain evidence that the product flow can work when its first-party assets load.

### Provider widget failure handling — 2026-10-29

- Package search now waits a bounded 20 seconds for the hydrated origin field after landing-page DOM load. If absent, it inspects only first-party failed request paths and returns a user-facing temporary-unavailable message when session-authentication failed; it explicitly says when no search was submitted. No cookies, tokens, or full request URLs are logged or returned.
- This handles the observed upstream `ERR_CONNECTION_CLOSED`; it cannot repair the provider service. Retry remains user initiated and does not resubmit a package query.

### User-initiated retry

- Vacation Packages UI now offers a retry button only for provider widget/service unavailable messages. It clears form fields and requires the traveler to re-enter explicit search values; the prior package request is never resubmitted automatically.

### Airport autocomplete clarity

- Updated live lookup messaging to distinguish verified Ignav airport selections from unresolved city/country/region text. Search continues to permit arbitrary geographic terms when airport lookup returns no matches or is unavailable, while disclosing incomplete airport coverage.

### Current state

- The project is `/home/rayyan/Projects/travel-hunter-pro`. Read `work.md` for the full product requirements and acceptance criteria; this implementation is still incomplete.

Its remaining work includes verified provider pricing/bookability, broader geographic coverage, infant and airline eligibility rules, deeper split-ticket search, live hotel/package validation, discount verification, and end-to-end acceptance evidence.

Credentialed partner calls are mocked unless credentials are present; Air Canada NDC has not been verified against an approved UAT/production environment.
- Expedia and Skyscanner consumer-page captures encountered CAPTCHA/DataDome challenges and were stopped. Integrations use their documented partner APIs; do not attempt to bypass challenges.
- Package search has a local-only Air Canada Vacations route and UI. The provider widget has intermittently failed to load because its first-party session-authentication request was connection-closed. The route reports temporary unavailability and does not auto-resubmit. A successful earlier probe is not current live verification of the app route.

- Historical test/build counts in this file are from their respective earlier checkpoints; rerun relevant checks after further changes. Never describe mocks/build success as provider-live verification.

### Recommended next work

1. Re-read the detailed requirements in `work.md` §§16–18 and keep `IMPLEMENTATION-STATUS.md` synchronized as acceptance evidence changes.
2. Finish airport and region semantics: resolve ambiguous cities/multi-airport areas clearly, support origin regions, and expose the limits of capped airport expansion.
3. Add verified live retrieval for hotel search and improve full-party/total-cost comparison. Keep unavailable or unverified providers explicitly unavailable.
4. Extend traveler rules for infants and carrier-specific eligibility, then deepen split-ticket search only where transfer time, baggage and mandatory costs can be compared.
5. Complete live package-route and UI verification when the provider widget is available; add only validated result details and preserve the explicit retry behavior.
6. Work through the remaining `work.md` acceptance criteria, recording test evidence and provider limitations in this handoff. Do not claim overall completion until each required criterion is either implemented and verified or explicitly resolved in the spec.

### Safety and data handling

- Keep browser automation local and user initiated. Do not reproduce, relay, or solve CAPTCHA challenges; stop and report the provider challenge when encountered.
- Keep `.env.local` and `.cache/vacation-probe/` private. Do not include credentials, cookies, tokens or session captures in commits, logs, or handoff excerpts.
- Preserve provenance: return only observations tied to the exact query and source, and avoid presenting estimates or listings as confirmed bookable prices.

## Origin region search — 2026-10-29

- `/api/search` now classifies origin and destination independently. The origin is automatically detected; the existing destination type selector remains available.
- When exactly one endpoint is a region, the backend resolves that region dynamically through Wikidata, substitutes up to the selected 3/5/10 candidate airports, queries the configured sources, applies the user's filters to the aggregate, and returns which endpoint was expanded plus each checked airport/source status.
- The results view identifies “Origin region” versus “Destination region.” Split-ticket optimization is disabled for origin-region results because its current API expects one airport code.
- Region-to-region searches currently return a clear 422 response. A proper pairwise strategy needs its own bounded search design so the app does not silently issue up to 100 provider searches or imply complete coverage.
- Added an origin-region aggregation regression test. Verification: `npm test` passes (66 tests) and `npm run build` passes. The build emits the existing Next.js warning that it ignored the project lockfile because the detected Turbopack root is `/home/rayyan`.
- This is bounded origin-region support, not completion of region-wide discovery: Wikidata coverage and the chosen candidate cap remain explicit limitations. Full `work.md` is still incomplete.

## Region-to-region airport-pair search — 2026-10-29

- Region searches now also support both endpoints being regions. The service resolves each region independently, takes up to the selected 3/5/10 candidate airports from each, and searches their Cartesian airport-pair set (at most 100 source searches for a 10-by-10 selection).
- Coverage reports both region records, airports selected, planned and completed pair counts, per-pair fare/source outcomes, and whether a block/rate-limit/auth status stopped the scan early. Results remain explicitly incomplete observations.
- Added a dedicated region coverage UI for pair searches. Fare refresh and split-ticket actions receive the observed airport route from each returned offer, not the broad region labels.
- Added tests for full bounded pair enumeration and early stop after a source block. Verification: 68 tests pass; production build passes; the local page responds HTTP 200. No provider-live search was made.
- Search breadth is bounded by the user-selected airport cap, and maximum 10-by-10 expansion can make up to 100 provider requests. Wikidata's returned candidate ordering and airport coverage are not a licensed or comprehensive directory. The remaining work in `work.md` is still substantial.

## Flight observation integrity and empty results — 2026-10-29

- Added `lib/flight-offer.mjs` as a shared observation validator for aggregated provider fares and the direct Google path. It checks CAD amounts, exact airport routes where the query uses airport codes, travel dates/times, cabin, quote timestamp, carrier names, connection codes, source metadata, and safe HTTPS source links. Unknown duration is permitted only as explicit null. Provider schema rejections are counted by provider/reason in `schemaCoverage` and shown as validation source statuses.

The normalizer now requires exact itinerary origin/destination, observed cabin, positive outbound/inbound duration and a usable quote timestamp/reference. ## Flight quote freshness — 2026-10-29

- Added `lib/quote-freshness.mjs`: flight observations are usable for at most 15 minutes under application policy, or until an earlier provider expiry when supplied. Timestamps over two minutes ahead of the application clock, missing/impossible timestamps and malformed expiry are rejected. This policy makes no claim about seller availability or guaranteed price expiry.
- Shared flight validation now excludes stale quotes. Direct Ignav search also uses the common gate, covering optimizer searches that bypass the provider aggregator. Unit fixtures now use current observation times instead of fixed historical times.
- Main flight cards use `QuoteGuard`, rechecking age every 15 seconds. Once stale, the card's price and booking/refresh/hotel actions are replaced with an instruction to run a fresh search.

Chromium with a simulated quote/clock confirmed a recent card rendered, then advancing 16 minutes removed all offer cards and source links with no page errors. No live provider requests were made for this check.

## City and airport search resolution — 2026-10-29

- Added `place-resolution.mjs`, `city-resolution.mjs`, and `location-search.mjs`. The main local search now resolves typed airport names/cities to exact airport scopes before querying code-only providers. IATA/metro code syntax passes to providers for verification; exact airport names use Ignav lookup. City scopes use a unique Wikidata settlement with country and coordinates, then airport P931 (place served) relationships and IATA codes.
- City names that identify multiple real settlements require an explicit airport selection. Same-country/name matches are not assumed to be one city. Fuzzy Ignav results cannot silently choose a destination. City lookup failure or missing served-city claims produces a clear selection error rather than fabricated codes.

- City, airport and region scopes use the bounded pair search; identical departure/arrival airport pairs are skipped. `requestedLocations` and `locationResolution` preserve user input and resolved provenance, and the UI shows city evidence plus returned candidates. The current airport cap still limits breadth. Split-party optimization is disabled for expanded geographic results, while exact offer actions retain the actual airport route.
- Explicit `source: google` place searches retain Google's existing city-name path. Local app `source: all` uses the new resolver; expanded searches require a same-origin local request. Typed three-letter codes no longer depend on a successful Wikidata classification request, and an explicit region-type selection still wins over code syntax.
- Live public Wikidata verification: Reykjavík resolved to Q1764 and RKV. The first attempt exposed a same-name Star Trek depiction; structured settlement/country/coordinate checks now exclude it. This returned airport set is incomplete coverage and is not a claim that all airports serving Reykjavík were enumerated.

## Durable flight queue and independent worker — 2026-10-29

- Main flight searches now use POST `/api/search/jobs` actions start/status/cancel. `lib/job-store.mjs` stores input, state, progress, result/error and lease ownership in private SQLite `.cache/jobs/search.sqlite` (optional shared `TRAVEL_JOB_DB` path). Unique request keys prevent duplicate enqueue; a reused key with different input is rejected. Queue capacity is 20 active jobs and the execution window is 15 minutes. Terminal records older than a day are pruned on subsequent submissions.
- `npm run worker` starts the independent worker, loading `.env.local`. Each job runs in an isolated child process using the same `executeFlightSearch` function as the retained synchronous API. The worker leases jobs for 30 seconds, renews ownership, fences late writes, and can reclaim expired leases up to three attempts. Cancellation revokes the lease and terminates the child at its next heartbeat. Read-only calls already received by a provider cannot be revoked; crash recovery may repeat them.
- Geographic searches persist airport-pair counts/progress. The frontend polls job status, exposes cancellation, and restores the saved job reference after reload without submitting a new search. Transport failures reconnect with bounded polling backoff. Missing/deleted job references report an error instead of polling forever. Origin/destination city-choice errors remain structured through the worker result.
- API-only validation checks payload size, party, filters, places, dates, cabin and same-origin localhost access before enqueue. The database is created mode 0600; search ages and provider session references must remain private. Worker concurrency is one per process; multi-host operation requires further infrastructure. Node >=22.13 is now declared in package metadata.
- Verification: the full 89-test suite and production build passed. A subsequent expired-queue-capacity test brought the total to 90; all seven affected queue/worker tests passed. Tests cover persistent reopen, request deduplication, stale-worker fencing, lease recovery, deadline/capacity handling, cancellation, progress and child-result persistence. Chromium confirmed one enqueue across a page reload and two status polls, with no page errors (intercepted provider/job responses).
- Real independent-worker checks: duplicate API starts returned the same job and an unsupported Google party failed before provider fetch. A live queued Ignav YHZ–YUL query for 2027-04-15 completed in one attempt with eight exact-route/date CAD observations accepted and persisted. No booking was made.
- Started worker process via exec session 25098; the web dev process was already active. Read README for two-process startup and shared-disk requirements. Do not mark P3 complete: hotel/browser and vacation jobs remain separate, and checkpoints/pause/resume, source-specific retry, authentication, monitoring and deployment gates remain unfinished.
- After confirming zero active jobs, restarted both services to load the final code: web exec session 67583, worker session 28402. HTTP 200 and the previously completed eight-offer job were confirmed after restart, demonstrating persistence across the actual service restart.

## Checkpointed flight pause/resume — 2026-10-29

- SQLite now migrates existing queues in place to add checkpoint snapshots and a separate crash-recovery counter. Paused jobs retain their original 15-minute deadline and count toward queue capacity; they can be resumed or cancelled. Repeated voluntary pauses do not consume the crash-recovery allowance. Lease revocation fences late progress/checkpoint/result writes.
- Worker children send checkpoint snapshots and wait for the parent's committed acknowledgement before proceeding. `searchLocations` saves resolved scopes/detections and each finished airport-pair result; recovery reuses those scopes and fresh completed pair results. Expired pair results are queried again. Final location aggregation excludes observations that have aged out during a long scan.
- Main form exposes Pause and Resume alongside Cancel. The paused job reference survives reload and reconnects without a new enqueue. A pause stops local execution at the next worker heartbeat; a provider request already received can still complete, and an in-flight uncheckpointed pair may repeat on resume. Explicit Google-only place queries still lack per-operation checkpoints.
- Verification: all 92 tests and production build passed. After a late-event shutdown guard change, the 16 affected queue/worker/location tests passed. Coverage includes interruption after the first pair, reuse without fresh location resolution, refreshing only a stale pair, checkpoint acknowledgement, and multiple pause/resume cycles without exhausting recovery.
- Chromium with intercepted job responses verified pause → reload → resume → result with one enqueue and no JavaScript errors. A real independent-worker same-airport diagnostic job completed with one persisted pair checkpoint; the adapter rejected identical airports before any provider fetch. No new live fare requests were made in this continuation.
- Restarted services with no active jobs: web exec session 57188 and worker session 12269. Full work.md is still incomplete; especially durable hotel/package workflows, per-source retries/rate limits, broader traveler/optimization rules, source/bookability verification and deployment/security remain open.

## Shared source pacing and HTTP failure handling — 2026-10-29

- Fixed a source-loop bug: REST adapters previously threw generic HTTP errors, and the aggregator mislabeled rate/access failures as ordinary errors. SourceRequestError/sourceFailure now preserve HTTP 401/403/429, temporary outages and retry times. Regional scans stop after these signals instead of continuing to later airport pairs.
- Added shared `source_policy` SQLite state and `fetchSource`. Atomic reservations pace per-provider request starts across processes (default 1000 ms, optional TRAVEL_SOURCE_GAP_MS). HTTP 429 persists at least a 60-second cooldown; auth/access refusals persist at least five minutes; longer Retry-After hints are respected. No credentials, URLs or bodies are stored in this policy table.
- Eligible read-only operations can retry a transient network/5xx failure once after backoff and pacing. HTTP 401/403/429 never auto-retry. Skyscanner session creation and Air Canada NDC POSTs are not replayed automatically. Google uses the paced fetch wrapper and preserves typed refusals even when API Anything wraps transport exceptions. Provider-reported Google blocks also persist cooldowns.
- Refresh, calendar, airport and seller-link routes return source status/retry-time diagnostics. Seller lookup now consistently requires same-origin localhost access. Search UI displays retry time; browser verification showed the rate-limit message with one enqueue and no JavaScript errors.
- Verification: full 98-test suite/build passed. Two later focused tests brought the repository to 100 cases; all 16 affected source/search tests and the final build passed. Coverage includes Retry-After formats, cross-connection pacing/cooldowns, no network call during cooldown, safe retry bounds, non-replayed POSTs, API error headers and an actual Ignav-adapter HTTP 429 stopping a regional scan after one pair. All provider failures were injected; no live fare requests or deliberate provider rate-limit probes were made.
- After verifying no queued/running/paused jobs, reloaded services: web session 32081, worker 72619. Webpage HTTP 200 confirmed. Remaining work includes hotel/package durability/policy, complete source coverage and traveler/optimization rules, authentication/deployment/monitoring and the remaining work.md acceptance gates. Full objective is still incomplete.

## Durable vacation-package jobs — 2026-10-29

- The queue now supports `kind: flight | package` with an in-place SQLite migration. Product kind is part of request-key identity, and legacy rows default to flight. Shared worker dispatch loads the package adapter only for package jobs. `npm run worker` handles both products.
- Package starts validate the explicit source labels, airport codes, dates and adult/room occupancy before enqueue. The package UI uses the shared `useSearchJob` hook with its own saved job reference; progress, cancellation, result/error and reload restoration are independent of flight jobs. Package cards use the observation-age guard.
- POST `/api/packages/search` now returns HTTP 202 with a queued job reference. It accepts Idempotency-Key; poll the general jobs endpoint for results. It no longer executes Chromium inside the web request.
- A package lease is serialized across workers. Cancellation propagates to the browser adapter and closes Chromium; worker dispatch waits for child exit during cancellation, and the database holds a seven-second cross-worker cleanup slot. Lost package browser leases become terminal errors on claim/status polling and are never automatically replayed. Pause/resume is intentionally rejected for browser package sessions; users can cancel and explicitly start a fresh search.
- Added package validation/progress/cancellation handling and tests for kind isolation, cross-worker serialization, lost-session behavior, cleanup spacing and browser closure/lock release. Full 103-test suite and production build passed, followed by all 15 affected checks after final lifecycle improvements (and eight queue checks after the final polling assertion).

It ended with “Air Canada Vacations search form did not load. No package search was submitted.” The error was persisted; no package quotes or booking were claimed. A subsequent process inspection found no remaining package runner/Chromium process.

Remaining scope includes live widget/result verification, better package location selection and child/infant occupancy, durable hotel jobs, other source integrations and remaining work.md acceptance requirements. ## Durable ticket comparison jobs — 2026-10-29

## Exact observed fare calendar — 2026-10-29

- Replaced UI cheapest-date indications with exact dated Ignav fare queries. Each date preserves the airport route, trip type, cabin, actual party/child ages and all flight filters. Round-trip return departure is the outbound departure plus the selected number of nights; this is departure-relative trip length, not independently verified hotel nights. Lowest prices come from actual matching itineraries; no date prices are generated by formulas. Sorting the main flight results high-to-low does not change calendar selection of the lowest matching quote per date.
- `calendar-query.mjs` validates exact airports, explicit counts/ages, dates, trip type/cabin, stay length and a bounded request budget. Ranges include up to 31 departure dates. Default budget is 62, maximum 100; each initial date needs one fare lookup. `calendar-search.mjs` checks CAD cent precision, source identity, quote freshness, actual segment routes/times and both directions before applying filters. It preserves each selected itinerary, source, timestamp, fee limitation and seller reference.
- Added `kind: calendar` queue dispatch, progress, idempotency, pause/resume, cancellation, reload restoration and the normal deadline/lease/recovery bounds. `/api/search/calendar` now returns HTTP 202 and a job reference. General `/api/search/jobs` also starts calendars. The old `getIgnavCalendar` helper remains a lower-level supplier-indication operation and no longer supplies UI prices.
- Extracted shared `fare-session.mjs` from comparison execution. Calendar and comparison jobs acknowledge request intents before provider reads and persist completed responses before advancing. Fresh completed dates are reused on resume; interrupted reads and stale refreshes consume the same persisted budget. Old comparison checkpoint shape/signatures remain supported. Source refusals stop later dates while retaining fresh earlier quotes, with explicit not-checked cells and retry evidence. Rejected source/application quotes, expired quotes and no-match dates remain distinct from unscanned dates.
- The browser restores an untouched flight form from the user's actual saved calendar query. Selecting a date loads its route, party, cabin and complete filter snapshot into the flight form. Saved-query details remain visible if the draft differs. Currency labels explicitly say CAD. Each cell has itinerary/provenance details and seller lookup; expiry hides amounts/actions. Mobile QA found a pre-existing overflowing location-mode row; wrapping it removed horizontal overflow at 390px.
- Verification: full 151-test suite and production build passed. All nineteen affected calendar/comparison API, engine and actual forked-worker checks passed after the final rejection/currency refinements, followed by a final passing build. Cases cover a full 31-day month/return-month boundary, children/youth ages, business cabin, all filters, actual inbound connection countries, invalid/expired prices, early quote expiry, provider refusal, acknowledged recovery and budget preservation. Build retains the existing home-directory lockfile warning; Node route tests also emit the existing module-type warning.

It also verified explicit CAD labels, mobile width and no JavaScript errors. Only this controlled QA uses fixtures; production has no fixture fallback.

The one-way job `0804ad12-b26a-4e6d-84d1-76097bb78362` returned two matching CAD 300 observations. ## Official hotel source continuation — 2026-10-29

- Added an optional **Official hotel website** source to hotel search, alongside the existing Booking.com source. Users can choose Booking.com, official sites, or both. Official-site queries require actual adult ages, stay dates and occupancy; the UI does not invent guest ages. The source can be limited to a user-entered official URL or use bounded discovery.
- Official-site discovery queries OpenStreetMap around the resolved destination (10 km, four/five-star hotel records, at most five candidates by default and a user-selectable cap). It requires a live city identity and coordinates, keeps OSM's candidate records as discovery evidence only, and displays OpenStreetMap attribution. This is a bounded candidate search, not a complete directory or proof of official status.
- The site inspector checks structured hotel identity/location and star evidence, then accepts a published Reservit booking identity only when one unambiguous supported hotel ID is present. A booking-site URL is not treated as evidence by itself. Non-Reservit engines such as Mews, TravelClick/SynXis, and other unsupported systems are not queried by this adapter.
- Added a direct integration for Reservit's documented public `bestPrice` endpoint through the local API Anything runtime. Requests include the actual stay, room and adult ages, language, and requested CAD currency. No partner ID is sent. The result must explicitly identify CAD and match the expected numeric price; a different or ambiguous currency is rejected without conversion. `-1` is treated as unavailable under the endpoint's documented semantics, not as proof the property is sold out.
- Official direct quotes are whole-stay observations only. The UI preserves their source text and direct booking link, and leaves room/rate name, included taxes, cancellation terms and final checkout amount unknown unless the source provides them. Flight-plus-hotel subtotal still requires fresh, matching stay/occupancy and verified property-location evidence; an unverified OSM address cannot establish a flight/hotel location match.
- Added strict public HTTPS fetching for this source: public-address and DNS checks, DNS-pinned TLS requests, redirect revalidation, bounded response size/time, and no cookies, credentials or browser-session forwarding. City resolution can now resolve a settlement without requiring an airport, which is needed for standalone hotels and small destinations.
- Verification code now covers the public-fetch restrictions, OSM candidate bounds, Reservit identity and CAD parsing, actual guest-age propagation through API Anything, unsupported-room refusal, rate-limit metadata, partial results, cancellation, property/stay subtotal integrity, municipality resolution, and an explicitly intercepted child-worker path. Test fixtures intercept network traffic; they are not production fallbacks. Full-suite/build/browser verification for this continuation must be recorded after its final run.
- **Live source limitation:** the official Hostellerie de l'Abbaye de la Celle Reservit endpoint returned `500,00 €` after a request that explicitly asked for CAD. The adapter correctly rejects it. A separate official Merchant Hotel booking form also rejected CAD and defaulted to GBP. These checks establish currency rejection behavior, not a live CAD hotel quote or current availability. No official-hotel CAD price has been verified.
- Other checked Canadian properties used TravelClick/SynXis or unsupported booking engines; none was added as a working adapter. A stale Mews/reservit identifier was not reused as current property evidence. Official-source coverage remains limited to the supported Reservit path and current live discovery metadata.
- Remaining hotel work: verify a real supported property that returns explicit CAD, confirm live address/identity and exact stay/occupancy behavior, test the browser UI against that live observation, and add other booking engines only through supported documented interfaces. Multi-room/child official pricing, full all-in costs, live Booking.com extraction, complete hotel coverage and checkout confirmation remain open. Do not describe this feature or `work.md` as complete.

## ITA Matrix adapter implementation — 2026-10-29

- Added `lib/ita-matrix.mjs` and wired it into `lib/providers.mjs` as the optional `ita-matrix` flight source. It discovers the public web-app key from the official Matrix landing page/same-origin JS assets, caches it in `.cache/ita-matrix/api-key.json` with mode 0600 for up to 30 days, and accepts an optional server-side `ITA_MATRIX_API_KEY` override. The search builds Matrix's separate backend multipart payload and SPA `/flights`/`/calendar` URLs, validates inputs, uses shared source pacing/cooldown/timeout handling, and originally used a five-minute process-local fare cache; that response cache was replaced on 2026-11-08 by the shared SQLite cache described above.
- It normalizes only explicit CAD prices and matching one-flight-per-direction itineraries with offset-bearing departure/arrival timestamps and a single identified carrier. Other response shapes, connections, unknown currency, and incomplete offers are withheld; children, youths, infants, and identified parties are not searched through Matrix until age/eligibility handling is evidenced. Matrix URLs are research links, not booking links. Successful normalized API responses now use the shared five-minute SQLite cache documented above; browser Matrix family/infant searches bypass it.
- Offline tests cover URL/body differences for round trips, youth-vs-child request fields, premium cabin, multipart JSON parsing, CAD price validation, strict itinerary mapping, cross-process cache, missing key, and access-denied behavior. These are contract fixtures based on the public reverse-engineering model, not a captured live Matrix response. The cross-process cache regression uses `searchMatrix` against two child processes and disposable SQLite storage.
- Key-selection correction: Matrix's linked Alkali bundle contains keys for multiple Google services/environments. The resolver now selects the explicitly named production `matrix` key rather than the first API-key-looking string, and ignores old unversioned cache entries. A live read-only query (JFK–YYZ, 2026-11-15 to 2026-11-22, one adult, CAD) returned 16 provider solutions; nine complete matching nonstop offers passed normalization. Seven incomplete solutions were initially withheld; the price-only display below now surfaces matching explicit-CAD totals separately. This verifies key discovery, the request path and a narrow parser case; it is not seller confirmation or booking. No user itinerary was searched.
- Unified Matrix results: route/date-matching solutions with an explicit CAD total but incomplete segment/carrier detail appear in the same price-sorted results list as fully validated itineraries, with a distinct “ITA Matrix price only” label. These observations are excluded from filters, split comparisons, hotel add-ons, and booking actions; unknown-currency and route/date-mismatched totals remain unpriced. Regression tests cover a connecting/multi-carrier response and mismatched route/currency refusal.
- Current loopback `/api/search` smoke check returned nine verified Matrix itineraries plus seven explicit-CAD price-only observations for the same one-adult JFK–YYZ round trip; all seven had finite amounts and were rendered separately in the result contract. `npm test -- --test-concurrency=2` passed 205 tests and `npm run build` passed. These are observations, not seller-confirmed prices.
- Verification after the correction: `npm test -- --test-concurrency=2` passed all 204 tests and `npm run build` passed. A loopback request through the running Next.js `/api/search` route completed the same one-adult JFK–YYZ round-trip query with HTTP 200 and nine Matrix observations. The private key cache is mode 0600. The existing Next.js warning about ignoring `/home/rayyan/package-lock.json` remains. `npm audit` was not rerun.
- The endpoint is unofficial and reverse-engineered; use only with confirmed permission. Broader response variations, connections, seller availability, and UI end-to-end behavior remain unverified. The fare cache is process-local and does not carry across forked worker jobs.

## Europe airport ranking, configurable scope, and stale-cache fix — 2026-10-31

The prior Europe diagnosis said candidates were alphabetic and the default was three. Current code inspection found the implementation had since changed: the resolver orders M49 airports by highest recorded Wikidata P3872 passenger figure, the UI default was already 10, and the code had a custom airport-limit input. However, `.cache/regions-v5` still contained the previous resolver's alphabetic Europe list (`AAA, AAL, AAQ…`), and the UI helper incorrectly said `1–100` while the server accepted `4–100`. This persisted stale result explains why the change did not appear for the user.

The resolver now uses `.cache/regions-v6`, so results from the alphabetic resolver cannot mask popularity ordering. The airport-limit control clearly says 4–20 candidates, defaults to 10, and sends the user's selected amount. Server validation enforces the same 4–20 range. A two-region search therefore has a hard maximum of 400 planned pairs; it still stops early on source blocks/rate limits and reports omitted pairs. Resolver ranking means highest *recorded* Wikidata passenger count, not necessarily current traffic; airports without a figure are placed after ranked airports in IATA order. The continental-Europe geographic filter remains an approximate bounding box applied to M49 country members, so it is not an authoritative airport-level boundary.

Added tests for defaulting to 10 (above the earlier three-airport behavior), respecting a custom six-airport selection, sorting a shuffled passenger-count fixture, and refusing a 21-airport search before resolver/provider calls. Focused verification: `node --test tests/search.test.mjs tests/region-cache.test.mjs` passed 15/15. A new live `lookupRegion('Europe')` attempt failed with `Wikidata region lookup is unavailable or timed out` after 27.2 seconds; no fresh live popularity list or fare search was obtained. Run full test/build/audit before relying on this change. The global work.md audit remains open.

Final verification for the airport-selection change: `npm test -- --test-concurrency=2` passed 297/297; `npm run build` passed; `npm audit` found 0 vulnerabilities. The live Wikidata resolver check timed out as noted above. No browser fare search was performed, no fare evidence was obtained, and no booking or reservation was made. Next action: when upstream lookup succeeds, inspect the top 10 returned airports and observed P3872 values, then use the website with an exact route/date to record the actual selected and searched pairs.

## Europe airport ranking: live lookup and website submission verified — 2026-10-31

The first popularity-query design did not meet the live requirement: its grouped join across 51 M49 countries exceeded the Wikidata timeout. A live probe then showed that Wikidata's `P30 Europe` country classification plus a continental coordinate filter can return passenger observations quickly. That query initially used only the exact airport class and omitted Heathrow/CDG because Wikidata models them as subclasses. The final Europe-specific resolver follows airport type subclass relationships (`P31/P279*`), reads direct passenger traffic property P3872, collapses repeated traffic statements to the highest recorded value per IATA code, and sorts descending by that value. It caps raw observations at 1,000 and airport candidates at 100; the UI and search logic use the selected 4–20 (default 10). A 20-airport cap bounds two-region Cartesian searches to 400 pairs. Europe results are isolated in `.cache/regions-v7` so earlier alphabetic lists cannot persist.

Fresh live `resolveRegion('Europe')` completed in 5.1 seconds and returned 100 ranked airports, 247 further ranked airport codes, 49 countries represented in the capped observations, and `moreAvailable: true`. Its top observed values were IST 84,457,421; LHR 83,884,572; CDG 67,421,316; AMS 66,828,453; MAD 66,197,066; FRA 61,564,957; FCO 51,306,744; SAW 48,407,318; BCN 41,639,622; MUC 41,568,000. These are Wikidata's highest recorded values, not synchronized current annual traffic. Scope uses Wikidata country-to-continent membership (Q46) and an approximate box (25°W–45°E, 34°N–72°N); it can include transcontinental countries and excludes airports with no recorded P3872 value or coordinates. The observation cap and geography are coverage limitations, not exhaustive Europe.

The rendered built website was checked with Chromium. It showed default 10, allowed custom 14, and displayed 196 airport pairs for two region endpoints. A second intercepted browser check (no real fare request) verified the website submitted `airportLimit: 14` to `/api/search/jobs` with the selected Europe/Canada endpoints. This confirms the custom value reaches the backend boundary; it does not evidence fare availability.

Verification after the final query/type changes: 297 tests passed; `npm run build` passed; `npm audit` found 0 vulnerabilities. Europe ranking was also verified against live Wikidata as above. No flight supplier search or purchase was made. Overall `work.md` remains incomplete.
